- Added production memory component logs: `current_frontier_bytes`, `solved_bytes`, `scratch_pool_bytes`, and `frontier_state_capacity`. The logged `static_allocation_bytes` equals `current_frontier_bytes + aligned solved bytes + max(layout_streams_bytes, layout_final_bytes)`, so `layout_streams` and `layout_final` still overlay one `scratch_pool`.
- Production tuning logs saved under `test_results/`: default `STREAM3_RING_SLOTS=16` depth20 took `189.306s`; tuned `STREAM3_RING_SLOTS=32,threshold_period=64` depth20 took `115.334s`; `STREAM3_RING_SLOTS=64` failed at depth5 due final spill flush non-convergence and left only about `1.14GB` free after static allocation.
- Nsight Systems node profile for tuned depth8 saved as `test_results/nsys_s3slots32_p64_d8_2026-05-23.nsys-rep` and `.sqlite`. Kernel analysis: Stream1 union `12.109617s`, Stream3/threshold/final union `3.314409s`, Stream4 slots union `0.889611s`; Stream1 overlap with Stream3 and Stream4 was `0s` in the current scheduler.
- Nsight bottleneck finding: `stream1_folded_input_kernel` consumed `9.678188s`, while CUTLASS GEMM kernels consumed `1.967323s` in the tuned depth8 profile. Current Stream1 bottleneck is pre-GEMM state-to-feature folding, not TensorCore GEMM.
- Verification after tuning/logging changes: `production_runner 0 1 4096` passed with new memory logs, and Docker CUDA/CUTLASS/NCCL `ctest` passed 12/12.
- Architecture decision fixed: next scheduler implementation should be an event-driven production scheduler with ring-slot/ring/shard state machines, CUDA events, ready queues, and no wave-level `cudaStreamSynchronize` barriers before the final `layout_final` transition. Required validation is Nsight overlap where Stream1 overlaps with Stream3 and Stream4.
- Stream1 bottleneck note fixed: `stream1_folded_input_kernel` currently materializes the first hidden layer from sparse state bytes by summing `input_weight[(position, state_value), hidden]` plus bias for every `(parent, hidden1)` pair. This path performs irregular indexed reads and scalar fp32 accumulation before TensorCore GEMMs; therefore the pre-GEMM feature folding dominated the depth8 profile.
- Replaced the old Stream1 folded input CUDA kernel with `stream1_folded_input_half2_tiled_kernel`. The new kernel loads `State128` into shared memory once per `(parent, hidden-tile)` block and computes two hidden columns per thread with `half2` loads/stores, while keeping CUTLASS TensorOp GEMMs for dense layers. cuBLAS/CUTLASS dense one-hot expansion was not used because it would multiply compute/memory and violate the fixed-memory architecture.
- Stream1 benchmark after folded-input optimization: best `B_MICRO=8192, concurrent=1` improved to about `45.07M candidates/sec`; best measured table value was about `48.14M candidates/sec` at `B_MICRO=4096, concurrent=3`.
- Nsight after folded-input optimization: old folded kernel `9.678188s` became `stream1_folded_input_half2_tiled_kernel=3.232160s` on the same depth8 profile shape, about `2.99x` faster. CUTLASS GEMM time was `1.786932s`.
- Production after folded-input optimization: `production_runner 0 8 4194304` completed with `elapsed_sec=9.94228`, `avg_depth_sec=1.24278`, depth6 `4.0061s`, depth7 `3.91021s`. Profiled depth8 run completed with `elapsed_sec=10.1268`.
- Production after folded-input optimization: `production_runner 0 20 4194304` completed with `elapsed_sec=60.6226`, `avg_depth_sec=3.03113`, depth19 `4.79004s`, improving over the previous tuned depth20 `115.334s`.
- Verification after folded-input optimization: Docker CUDA/CUTLASS/NCCL `ctest` passed 12/12; guard scans show no active forbidden fallback/PyTorch/smoke/odd-even/bitonic/hash-table terms, and atomics remain only in Stream2 solved path.
- Replaced the wave-synchronous depth scheduler with an event-driven ring state machine. `run_depth_cuda_graphs` now uses per-ring CUDA events, ring states `Free/Stream1Running/ReadyForStream3/Stream3Running`, and free-ring relaunching instead of wave-level `cudaStreamSynchronize(stream1)` barriers. Stream3 launch count now follows actual non-empty rings, so dispatcher test expectations were updated from wave count to `ceil(parent_jobs / RING_SLOT_COUNT)`.
- Added event-driven Stream4 sort-slot management in the depth loop with host-side shard processing ownership and per-shard metadata copies for non-processing shards, preserving the no-atomic Stream3/Stream4 contract. Stream4 is still drained after each two-ring Stream3 group to keep threshold cadence and spill convergence stable.
- Event-driven scheduler validation: `production_runner 0 20 4194304` completed with `elapsed_sec=63.9526`, `avg_depth_sec=3.19763`, full-beam `stream3_jobs=17`, `stream4_jobs=576`, and `threshold_updates=133`. This is slightly slower than the folded-input wave scheduler depth20 run (`60.6226s`) but removes the Stream1 wave barrier and preserves stable threshold behavior.
- Nsight event-scheduler profile saved as `test_results/nsys_event_scheduler_d8_2026-05-23.nsys-rep` and `.sqlite`. Measured overlap: `Stream1 x Stream3 = 0.618212s`; `Stream1 x Stream4 = 0s` in the stable implementation because Stream4 is drained before next Stream3 for spill safety and cadence. Experimental Stream1-before-Stream4 overlap variants were rejected because they broke depth5/depth6 spill convergence.
- Verification after event-driven scheduler: Docker CUDA/CUTLASS/NCCL `ctest` passed 12/12. Guard scans show no removed fallback/PyTorch/smoke/odd-even/bitonic/hash-table terms and atomics remain only in Stream2 solved path.
- Tested `RING_COUNT=4` event-scheduler variants. `BEAM_RING_COUNT=4` and `BEAM_RING_COUNT=4,BEAM_GLOBAL_THRESHOLD_UPDATE_PERIOD_SHARDS=32` both failed final spill flush convergence at depth9 with static allocation about `5.06GB`; these variants were not adopted.
- Added Stream3-owned GPU ready-queue handoff for Stream4 scheduling. New fixed `layout_streams` arrays are `stream3_ready_flag`, `stream3_ready_shard_list`, and `stream3_ready_count`. `stream3_build_ready_shard_queue_cuda` marks ready shards and sets `processing_flag` on GPU without atomics; host dispatcher consumes only the ready shard list and no longer writes Stream4 `processing_flag` during normal scheduling.
- Dispatcher now can enqueue the next free Stream1 rings after the first batch of ready Stream4 sort-slot launches while preserving spill safety, because Stream3 has already set `processing_flag` before host launches Stream4 graphs. Final spill flush uses the same ready queue with `force_dirty_flush=true`.
- Verification after Stream3 GPU ready-queue handoff: Docker CUDA/CUTLASS/NCCL `ctest` passed 12/12. Guard scan showed atomics only in Stream2 solved path.
- Production after Stream3 GPU ready-queue handoff: `production_runner 0 20 4194304` completed with `elapsed_sec=59.5912`, `avg_depth_sec=2.97956`, `last_final_frontier_size=4194501`, `last_final_threshold=7116`, and `threshold_updates=245`.
- Nsight after Stream3 GPU ready-queue handoff saved as `test_results/nsys_stream3_ready_queue_d8_2026-05-23.nsys-rep` and `.sqlite`. CUDA graph trace overlap: Stream1 ring graphs union `6389.008ms`, Stream4 slots union `2886.652ms`, Stream1 x Stream4 overlap `1439.183ms`, Stream1 x Stream3 overlap `142.065ms`, Stream3 x Stream4 overlap `0ms`.
- Changed production ring/scheduler policy to match the score/hash ring architecture: default `RING_COUNT=4`, score/hash ring is freed immediately after Stream3 consumes it, and Stream4 scheduling uses a host metadata-only `pending_stream4_shards` queue. Stream4 no longer blocks ring reuse; pending shards drain for periodic threshold update and final depth flush.
- Verification after ring4 nonblocking Stream4 scheduler: Docker CUDA/CUTLASS/NCCL `ctest` passed 12/12. Guard scans show atomics only in Stream2 solved path and no active fallback/smoke/PyTorch/torch/EmbeddingBag/Stream4 hash-table/bitonic/odd-even code.
- Production after ring4 nonblocking Stream4 scheduler: `production_runner 0 20 4194304` completed with `elapsed_sec=57.5207`, `avg_depth_sec=2.87603`, `last_final_frontier_size=4195495`, `last_final_threshold=6931`, and `threshold_updates=120`. Static allocation is `5061596416` bytes, with `2399141888` bytes free after static allocation on RTX 3070 Laptop GPU.
- Nsight after ring4 nonblocking Stream4 scheduler saved as `test_results/nsys_ring4_nonblocking_stream4_d8_2026-05-23.nsys-rep` and `.sqlite`. CUDA graph trace overlap: Stream1 ring graphs union `6536.535ms`, Stream4 slots union `2738.789ms`, Stream3/threshold/final union `3041.616ms`, Stream1 x Stream4 overlap `1059.274ms`, Stream1 x Stream3 overlap `422.207ms`, Stream3 x Stream4 overlap `1125.344ms`.
- Replaced Stream3 collector `SHARD_COUNT * input_count` scans with a partition-by-shard path. Stream3 now fills fixed `(shard_id, CandidateMeta)` scratch, uses CUB radix sort by shard id, uses CUB reduce-by-key for batch counts, and scatters contiguous shard transactions into `survivor_shard` or ping-pong spill buffers without atomics.
- Added fixed pre-start Stream3 partition scratch arrays in `layout_streams`: `stream3_partition_key_a/b`, `stream3_partition_val_a/b`, `stream3_partition_unique_shard`, `stream3_partition_unique_counts`, and `stream3_partition_unique_count`.
- Verification after Stream3 partition collector: Docker CUDA/CUTLASS/NCCL `ctest` passed 12/12. Guard scans show atomics only in Stream2 solved path and no active fallback/smoke/PyTorch/torch/EmbeddingBag/Stream4 hash-table/bitonic/odd-even code.
- Production after Stream3 partition collector: `production_runner 0 20 4194304` completed with `elapsed_sec=53.1457`, `avg_depth_sec=2.65728`, `last_final_frontier_size=4196489`, `last_final_threshold=7003`, and `threshold_updates=112`. Static allocation is `5514581760` bytes, with `1946157056` bytes free after static allocation on RTX 3070 Laptop GPU.
- Nsight after Stream3 partition collector saved as `test_results/nsys_stream3_partition_collector_d8_2026-05-23.nsys-rep` and `.sqlite`. CUDA graph trace overlap: Stream1 ring graphs union `6040.549ms`, Stream4 slots union `2574.652ms`, Stream3/threshold/final union `2246.569ms`, Stream1 x Stream4 overlap `1023.040ms`, Stream1 x Stream3 overlap `484.731ms`, Stream3 x Stream4 overlap `1063.076ms`.
- Reworked the production depth scheduler to remove CPU busy polling. Ring completion now uses `stream1_running_rings` and `stream3_ready_rings` FIFO queues; Stream4 sort-slot completion now uses `stream4_free_slots` and `stream4_busy_slots` FIFO queues. The common path queries only the oldest in-flight event and uses `cudaEventSynchronize` only for backpressure/drain.
- Preserved ring ownership semantics after scheduler cleanup: Stream3 launches from completed rings, and score/hash rings are freed only after Stream3 completion. Stream4 pending shards can launch while Stream3 is active.
- Restored stable threshold convergence after the polling cleanup by keeping the threshold boundary as a pending-Stream4 drain point, adding clean-full shard final flush jobs, and adding spill-aware monotonic threshold tightening during final flush.
- Verification after scheduler polling cleanup: Docker CUDA/CUTLASS/NCCL `ctest` passed 12/12. Guard scans show atomics only in Stream2 solved path and no active fallback/smoke/PyTorch/torch/EmbeddingBag/Stream4 hash-table/bitonic/odd-even code.
- Production after scheduler polling cleanup: `production_runner 0 20 4194304` completed with `elapsed_sec=55.2659`, `avg_depth_sec=2.7633`, `last_final_frontier_size=4194726`, `last_final_threshold=22721`, and `threshold_updates=69`. This is about `4.0%` slower than the previous Stream3 partition collector run, but it removes CPU busy polling.
- Nsight after scheduler polling cleanup saved as `test_results/nsys_scheduler_polling_cleanup_d8_2026-05-23.nsys-rep` and `.sqlite`. CUDA API `cudaEventQuery` calls dropped from `12114605` in the previous Stream3 partition profile to `1996`; `cudaEventSynchronize` calls are now `301`. CUDA graph trace overlap: Stream1 ring graphs union `6678.836ms`, Stream4 slots union `576.322ms`, Stream3/threshold/final union `3844.815ms`, Stream1 x Stream4 overlap `246.486ms`, Stream1 x Stream3 overlap `1359.946ms`, Stream3 x Stream4 overlap `22.204ms`.
- Added fixed per-shard score histogram state in `layout_streams`: `shard_score_hist_a/b`, `shard_score_hist_active_index`, and `threshold_hist_active_snapshot`. Stream4 writes a complete inactive histogram after clean compact, flips the active histogram index, and clears `processing_flag` only after the histogram is valid.
- Moved periodic and final threshold computation to `streams.stream5`. Threshold build now sums active per-shard histograms instead of scanning `survivor_shard`; `threshold_sum_shard_histograms_kernel` took only `0.000935s` across 29 calls in the depth8 Nsight profile.
- Superseded the old Stream4 table-dedup clarification in `ARCHITECTURE_NEED.md`; current Stream4 implementation rule is CUB/fixed-temp threshold + compact + Hash128 sort/reduce + clean compact, with no Stream3/Stream4 atomics.
- No-wait Stream5 threshold experiment was rejected: `production_runner 0 20 4194304` without waiting active Stream4 slots completed with `elapsed_sec=60.6949`, `avg_depth_sec=3.03474`, slower than the stable barrier path because stale available histograms weakened periodic threshold tightening and added memory contention.
- Stable per-shard histogram production run completed: `production_runner 0 20 4194304` with `elapsed_sec=55.809`, `avg_depth_sec=2.79045`, `last_final_frontier_size=4194715`, `last_final_threshold=21377`, `threshold_updates=70`, `static_allocation_bytes=5244828416`, and `gpu_free_after_static_alloc_bytes=2216689664`.
- Nsight after per-shard histogram Stream5 threshold saved as `test_results/nsys_per_shard_hist_stream5_d8_2026-05-23.nsys-rep` and `.sqlite`. Depth8 run completed with `elapsed_sec=9.8143`, `avg_depth_sec=1.22679`; largest kernels are Stream1 folded input `3.497s`, CUTLASS GEMM `1.946s`, Stream3 scatter owner `1.761s`, and Stream2 hash/goal `0.690s`.
- Optimized Stream1 and Stream3 independently on real data. Stream1 folded input now uses `stream1_folded_input_half4_tiled_kernel`; Stream3 single-GPU path skips owner count/scatter, fuses restore with shard partition collection, and uses CUB `DeviceScan::ExclusiveSum` for block-count prefix.
- Isolated real-data benchmark after Stream1/Stream3 optimization: Stream1 best is `59.87M candidates/sec`; Stream3 `B_MICRO=8192`, `ring_slot_count=8` is `4.5892ms/job` and `342.73M candidates/sec`, improving from `17.4916ms/job` and about `89.9M candidates/sec`.
- Combined production run after Stream1/Stream3 optimization completed: `production_runner 0 20 4194304` with `elapsed_sec=45.9712`, `avg_depth_sec=2.29856`, `last_final_frontier_size=4194402`, `last_final_threshold=22928`, and `threshold_updates=69`. Report saved as `test_results/stream1_stream3_realdata_optimization_2026-05-23.md`.
- Nsight after Stream1/Stream3 optimization saved as `test_results/nsys_stream1_stream3_scan_opt_d8_2026-05-23.nsys-rep` and `.sqlite`. Top costs: Stream1 folded input `3.897s`, CUTLASS GEMM `2.004s`, Stream2 hash/goal `0.713s`, Stream4 merge/sort `0.480s`; Stream3 DeviceScan prefix is `0.000845s`.
- Verification after Stream1/Stream3 optimization: Docker CUDA/CUTLASS/NCCL `ctest` passed 12/12; guard scans show atomics only in Stream2 solved path and no active fallback/smoke/PyTorch/torch/EmbeddingBag/Stream4 hash-table/bitonic/odd-even code.
- Production retry for puzzle 7 with larger beam completed: `production_runner 7 200 8388608` solved in `8.36935s` with `solution_length=8` and `solution=BL.BL.BL.B.-F.-R.-FR.-L`. Log saved as `test_results/production_runner_p7_d200_b8388608_solution_2026-05-23.log`.
- Changed solved CSV/submission output contract: `submit.csv` and `test_results/submit_p*_d*_b*.csv` now write `initial_state_id,path` with generator sequence instead of `state120`; CPU validation state remains in solution logs only.
- Added production-run compile-time logging switches: `BEAM_ENABLE_DEPTH_LOGS` controls per-depth logs, `BEAM_ENABLE_DEBUG_LOGS` controls config/memory/debug logs, and the default fast build emits only base puzzle solved/unsolved status lines.
- Added Kaggle packaging under `kaggle/`: `beam_kernel.ipynb` has first-cell config for `BEAM_WIDTH`, `START_PUZZLE_ID`, `PUZZLE_COUNT`, depth/log flags/frequencies, and final-cell solved-length histogram PNG plus solved/min/max/average/mode statistics.
- Added `CUTLASS_DIR` CMake cache support for `/opt/cutlass`, source `external/cutlass`, and `/kaggle/working/cutlass`; missing CUTLASS is now a configure-time error instead of a fallback.
- Copied exported fp16 Stream1 weights from `build-docker/stream1_weights` to tracked `stream1_weights` for GitHub/Kaggle builds; default `production_runner` weight path is now `stream1_weights`, with explicit `BEAM_WEIGHT_DIR` override.
- Added `.gitignore` excluding build outputs, Docker build tree, profile artifacts, local `test_results`, PyTorch source weights, and generator zip while preserving source/data/exported fp16 weights.
- Verification after Kaggle packaging/log contract changes: Docker CUDA/CUTLASS/NCCL `ctest` passed 12/12; short `production_runner 0 1 1024` emitted only `puzzle_solved=0 puzzle_id=0 ...` in default fast-log mode.
- Resolved GitHub source URL for Kaggle from the existing Kaggle notebook: `https://github.com/TryDotAtwo/MultiGPUBeamSearch.git`; local `origin` and Kaggle notebook `GITHUB_REPO_URL` now point to this repository.
- Fixed first Kaggle run failure candidate by moving transient GitHub checkout, CUTLASS checkout, and CMake build tree from `/kaggle/working` to `/tmp`; `/kaggle/working` now stores only run logs, result CSV, submission CSV, and histogram PNG.
- Fixed Kaggle machine selection: `kaggle/kernel-metadata.json` now sets `machine_shape=NvidiaTeslaT4`; notebook cells now have explicit ids to remove nbformat MissingID warnings.
- Audited real-data Stream1 score direction with exported fp16 weights via CPU NumPy forward pass: for all 24 one-move-to-central synthetic parents, the solving move ranked `1/24` when lower `q` is better and `24/24` when higher `q` is better; current `score_key <= threshold` direction is consistent with the model.
- Verified real-data GPU end-to-end path after Kaggle packaging changes: `production_runner 7 20 8388608` solved with `solution_length=8`, `solution=BL.BL.-F.BL.B.-R.-FR.-L`, `seconds=8.25152`, `solved_count=2`, and CPU validation passed.
- Identified threshold architecture risk: dispatcher final-spill convergence currently calls periodic threshold with `reserved_width = GLOBAL_BEAM_WIDTH_EFFECTIVE - spill_reserve`, which tightens threshold against a target smaller than `GLOBAL_BEAM_WIDTH_EFFECTIVE` and can prune valid paths too aggressively; strict architecture should keep threshold target equal to `GLOBAL_BEAM_WIDTH_EFFECTIVE` and fix spill sizing/scheduling instead.
- Removed the final-spill `reserved_width` threshold path. `update_threshold_single_gpu` no longer accepts a beam-width override, so periodic and final threshold computation use `GLOBAL_BEAM_WIDTH_EFFECTIVE` exactly.
- Reworked `production_runner` config construction so default static buffers derive from `USER_GLOBAL_BEAM_WIDTH`, `WORLD_SIZE`, GPU free-memory budget/headroom, and `B_MICRO`. Derived fields now include shard count, Stream4 batch size, spill capacity, Stream3 ring slots, and `GLOBAL_BEAM_WIDTH_MAX_SAFE` capacity tail headroom. Explicit env vars remain override knobs.
- Added debug memory logs for derived config and `gpu_free_after_all_allocations_bytes`. Verification report saved as `test_results/beam_derived_config_2026-05-23.md`.
- Verification after beam-derived config and strict threshold changes: Docker CUDA/CUTLASS/NCCL `ctest` passed 12/12; short `production_runner 0 2 4194304` completed; real solved regression `production_runner 7 20 8388608` solved with `solution_length=7` and `solution_valid=1`.
- Added final-spill failure diagnostics in `cuda/dispatcher.cu`: on non-converged final Stream3 spill flush, the runner now prints per-shard clean/dirty/processing/ready counts, last Stream3 write/spill counts and offsets, active spill distribution by shard, and active-spill score min/max/average/pass/reject relative to `current_threshold`.
- Reduced single-GPU final layout memory by skipping the full `FinalResponse` buffer when `WORLD_SIZE=1`; `final_materialize` writes responses only when the response buffer is present, while still writing `next_frontier_states_tmp`.
- Added threshold filtering to Stream3 active-spill drain so spill entries above `current_threshold` do not recirculate during final spill convergence.
- Started local background rebuild of shared Docker image tags `gpu-dev-nsight-cutlass-cu128:2026-05-23` and `gpu-dev-nsight-cutlass-cu128:2026-05-22`; build logs are `test_results/docker_build_gpu_dev_2026-05-23.out.log` and `.err.log`.
- Replaced weak owner/shard modulo inputs with `hash128_distribution_key(Hash128)`, a deterministic SplitMix64-style finalizer over both halves of `Hash128`. Stream 3 device routing, host routing, and final-spill debug shard attribution now use the same distribution key; `Hash128` itself and dedup semantics are unchanged.
- Kaggle T4 validation after the distribution mixer change still failed at `puzzle_id=7` with the same final spill convergence signature: shards 0, 1, and 2 were full (`clean=147456`), active spill remained only on those shards (`236484` entries total), and all spill entries passed `current_threshold=17171`. Report saved as `test_results/kaggle_t4_v5_distribution_mixer_2026-05-23.md`.
- Reverted the distribution-mixer experiment and the extra active-spill threshold filtering so Stream3/Stream4 semantics stay unchanged. The final-spill debug dump remains because it is diagnostic-only on failure paths.
- Reworked `production_runner` runtime config sizing so `RING_COUNT` defaults to `2`, `STREAM4_BATCH_CANDIDATES` is derived before `SHARD_COUNT`, `SHARD_COUNT` is derived from local aligned frontier capacity and Stream4 batch, `STREAM3_BATCH_CANDIDATES = SHARD_COUNT * STREAM4_BATCH_CANDIDATES`, `RING_SLOT_COUNT = STREAM3_BATCH_CANDIDATES / (B_MICRO * MOVE_COUNT)`, and `GLOBAL_SPILL_CAPACITY` defaults to `STREAM3_BATCH_CANDIDATES`. Added a config guard requiring `STREAM3_BATCH_CANDIDATES` to be divisible by `B_MICRO * MOVE_COUNT`.
- Changed final equal-score tail policy: Stream1/2/3/4/5 may keep tails before finalization, but final filter/load-balance now caps `final_candidate_count` to `GLOBAL_BEAM_WIDTH_EFFECTIVE` after the final threshold scan and skips tail writes during final scatter. This preserves pre-final architecture behavior while forcing exact aligned beam size at final output.
- Added deterministic avalanche distribution keys for `owner_from_hash128` and `shard_from_hash128`. CPU code, CUDA Stream3 routing, and final-spill debug attribution now use the same domain-separated `Hash128` mixer before modulo `WORLD_SIZE` or `SHARD_COUNT`; raw `Hash128` remains the dedup key.
- Added final-local dedup support in the Stream3 ready-shard queue: normal pipeline behavior is unchanged, but final spill flush can now enqueue clean-only shards for Stream4 threshold+dedup+compact. This lets a card compact/dedup local shards after Stream1/2 are drained, before final global threshold and final cut.
- Changed final flush completion criterion: after final-local Stream4 compact/dedup, residual spill no longer blocks finalization when `dirty_count==0` and the clean deduped local candidate set already contains at least `GLOBAL_BEAM_WIDTH_EFFECTIVE` candidates. The final global threshold/cut then operates on the clean deduped set.
- Changed Kaggle notebook runtime config for a diagnostic-only T4 run: `BEAM_WIDTH=2**24`, `START_PUZZLE_ID=7`, `PUZZLE_COUNT=1`, `ENABLE_DEPTH_LOGS=True`, `ENABLE_DEBUG_LOGS=True`, and `DEPTH_LOG_EVERY=1`. This change is intended for Kaggle kernel push only, not GitHub source push.
- Changed Kaggle notebook runtime config for a 20-puzzle batch run: `BEAM_WIDTH=2**24`, `START_PUZZLE_ID=1`, `PUZZLE_COUNT=20`, `ENABLE_DEPTH_LOGS=False`, `ENABLE_DEBUG_LOGS=False`, `DEPTH_LOG_EVERY=1`, and `STOP_ON_FAILURE=False`. This change is intended for Kaggle kernel push only, not GitHub source push.
- Raised score quantization precision by changing `SCORE_SCALE` from `128` to `1024`; `CandidateMeta` and score key type remain unchanged, while score histogram bin count increases.
- Reworked production CPU history storage from full `CandidateMeta` records to compressed `HistoryEntry{parent_idx, route_packed}` records. RAM and disk history now store 16 bytes per candidate instead of 32 bytes per candidate.
- Added asynchronous RAM-history dead-branch pruning. After depth `d+1` is materialized on CPU, a background history worker compacts depth `d` to parent indices referenced by depth `d+1` and remaps `depth d+1` parent indices. `BEAM_HISTORY_WORKERS` controls concurrent pruning workers.
- Added Kaggle notebook config `HISTORY_WORKERS` and passes it to `BEAM_HISTORY_WORKERS`.
- Added optional known-solution survival tracing to `production_runner`. When `BEAM_TRACK_SOLUTION_PATH` is set, the runner hashes each expected prefix state and scans final per-depth `CandidateMeta` hashes to log whether the prefix survived the final threshold/cap. `BEAM_STOP_AFTER_TRACKED_PATH=1` stops a diagnostic run after the tracked path length when the solution was not found.
- Changed Kaggle notebook to pass known solution paths for puzzles 1 through 20, print `track_solution_*` lines, set `DEPTH_LIMIT=32` for survival diagnostics, and pass `BEAM_STOP_AFTER_TRACKED_PATH`.
- Fixed a `SCORE_SCALE=1024` threshold bug in Stream4 score histograms: score-key histogram radix sort now uses 32 score bits instead of 16, matching `SCORE_MAX_KEY=307200`. The old 16-bit sort split high score keys into non-contiguous groups and undercounted histogram bins, which allowed `final_threshold=UINT32_MAX` even when clean survivors exceeded the aligned beam.
- Changed final filter/load-balance selection to score-prioritized exact beam cutting: candidates with `score_key < final_threshold` are emitted first, then only the remaining aligned beam slots are filled with `score_key == final_threshold` candidates. Added a CUDA threshold test that would fail if layout-order capping drops a lower-score candidate.
- Added full tracked-solution path diagnostics. `production_runner` now logs `track_solution_prefinal` before final filtering and expanded `track_solution_prefix` after final filtering with matches, first/best index, score, threshold pass/margin, parent index, route fields, move name, and shard/local location where available.
- Added generated-candidate diagnostics before Stream3 thresholding. `run_depth_cuda_graphs` can now receive a tracked `(parent_idx, move)` request and logs `track_solution_generated` from score/hash rings after Stream1/2 finish and before Stream3 consumes the ring. This identifies whether a known path is lost in Stream1/2 generation, Stream3 threshold/dedup/collector, Stream4 threshold/dedup, or final filtering.
- Added Stream4 tracked-hash diagnostics. `production_runner` now logs `track_solution_stream4` with the tracked hash location after Stream3 collector (`clean`, `dirty`, `active_spill`, `inactive_spill`), Stream4 input threshold/pass state for the target shard job, and Stream4 output survival in the clean region.
- Added detailed tracked-parent Stream1 score diagnostics. For the tracked parent, `production_runner` now logs all 24 CUDA move scores/ranks and a host reference-forward score/rank table from the same exported fp16 weights and copied parent state. Added all-Stream4-event trace lines for the tracked shard and `BEAM_STOP_AFTER_TRACKED_MISSING_EXTRA_DEPTHS` to stop two depths after the first missing tracked prefix. Kaggle diagnostic config is now puzzle 0 only with the supplied 55-move tracked path.
- Kaggle version 24 diagnostic completed for puzzle 0 tracked path. The depth 6 tracked move `U` was generated by Stream1/2 with CUDA `score_key=307200`, while host reference-forward on the same parent/weights produced `score_key=22272`; report saved as `test_results/kaggle_puzzle0_stream1_cuda_reference_compare_2026-05-24.md`. Primary suspect is the Stream1 residual fc2 CUTLASS GEMM using `residual_tmp` as both input and output, which violates non-in-place GEMM assumptions.
- Fixed Stream1 residual buffer aliasing: residual fc1 writes to `scratch.residual_tmp`, residual fc2 now writes to an already allocated separate scratch buffer (`scratch.hidden1`/`scratch.hidden2` alternating) instead of overwriting its input. No new allocation, fallback, or runtime guard was added.
- Verified the Stream1 residual aliasing fix on Kaggle version 25. For puzzle 0 depth 6, expected move `U` changed from CUDA `score_key=307200` before the fix to CUDA `score_key=22288` after the fix, while host reference-forward produced `score_key=22272`; all 24 move ranks matched between CUDA and reference. Report saved as `test_results/kaggle_stream1_residual_fix_verification_2026-05-24.md`. Remaining tracked-path loss is now downstream of Stream1/2: the candidate is generated but not found after Stream3/spill routing.
- Added Stream3 tracked-candidate path diagnostics. `run_depth_cuda_graphs` now records whether the tracked hash exists after Stream3 threshold/dedup (`unique_*`), whether the tracked candidate exists in the Stream3 shard partition before scatter, and the target shard write/spill math (`group_offset`, `local_in_group`, `shard_write_count`, `shard_spill_offset`, `spill_idx`, `spill_capacity_drop`). `production_runner` logs this as `track_solution_stream3_path`.
- Removed the uncommitted Stream3 spill backpressure code from `cuda/dispatcher.cu` at user request. The current confirmed defect remains diagnostic-only: Kaggle version 26 showed a tracked candidate dropped because active global spill was full (`spill_idx=22544695`, `spill_capacity=16908288`, `spill_capacity_drop=1`). Any implementation fix must be discussed before code changes.
- Added a Stream3/Stream4 config-search derivation model. `GLOBAL_BEAM_WIDTH_MAX_SAFE` was removed; effective beam width is now only `round_up(USER_GLOBAL_BEAM_WIDTH, WORLD_SIZE * SHARD_COUNT * STREAM4_BATCH_CANDIDATES_PER_SHARD_UNIT)`. `STREAM3_BATCH_CANDIDATES` is derived from `BEAM_STREAM3_RING_SLOTS * B_MICRO * MOVE_COUNT`, `RING_COUNT` is derived from logical shard size, and `SHARD_COUNT/STREAM4_BATCH_CANDIDATES` are selected under `layout_streams_bytes <= layout_final_budget_bytes` and GPU memory budget.
- Added `StaticMemoryPlan.layout_final_budget_bytes` so production config search can compare stream-layout memory against the final-stage budget without using the previous self-expanding `layout_final_bytes` value.
- Kaggle version 28 verified the config-search build on T4 but failed fast through the existing Stream3 spill fatal guard. The selected config used `STREAM4_BATCH_CANDIDATES=1398784` and `GLOBAL_SPILL_CAPACITY=393216`; shard 3 was still `processing_flag=1`, active spill was already `388323/393216`, and a new `32593`-candidate spill group exceeded capacity. Report saved as `test_results/kaggle_v28_config_search_spill_guard_2026-05-24.md`.
- Updated the production config-search objective after the Kaggle v28 spill guard failure. Default spill capacity now uses the architecture backlog bound `max(2*STREAM3_BATCH_CANDIDATES, STREAM4_ACTIVE_SORT_SLOTS*LOGICAL_SHARD_SIZE)`, and candidate scoring now balances Stream4 waves, Stream4 jobs, Stream4 batch units, jobs per logical shard, and shards per sort slot. Added knobs `BEAM_MIN_STREAM4_JOBS_PER_SHARD`, `BEAM_TARGET_STREAM4_JOBS_PER_SHARD`, and `BEAM_TARGET_SHARDS_PER_SORT_SLOT`.
- Kaggle version 29 caught an NVCC-only compile issue in the new spill bound expression: `std::max` received mixed `unsigned long long` and `unsigned long` operands. Fixed the expression by materializing the concurrent shard backlog as `std::uint64_t`.
- Kaggle version 30 showed the same NVCC deduction issue remained because `2ULL` still forced `unsigned long long`; the spill bound expression now uses `std::max<std::uint64_t>` explicitly.
- Kaggle version 31 built successfully and exposed a resident-window flow problem: `SHARD_COUNT=7`, `STREAM4_BATCH_CANDIDATES=799744`, and `N_LOCAL=16780288`; Stream3 hit the fatal guard when shard 1 reached `clean_count=1599488 == 2*STREAM4_BATCH_CANDIDATES`. The rejected invariant `SHARD_COUNT*2*STREAM4_BATCH_CANDIDATES >= N_LOCAL` is intentionally not in code because resident Stream4 shard storage is a processing window, not full logical-shard storage. The remaining fix must address scheduler/threshold/backlog flow.
- Decoupled resident shard capacity from Stream4 batch sizing. `RuntimeConfig` now has `SHARD_CAPACITY_CANDIDATES`, `SHARD_CAPACITY_SCALE_PPM`, `STREAM4_BATCH_ALIGNMENT`, and `GLOBAL_SPILL_SCALE_PPM`; `survivor_shard` is allocated as `SHARD_COUNT * SHARD_CAPACITY_CANDIDATES`, while Stream4 batch remains the dirty-count launch threshold. Production config search derives shard capacity from `LOGICAL_SHARD_SIZE * SHARD_CAPACITY_SCALE_PPM` and derives default spill as `STREAM4_ACTIVE_SORT_SLOTS * STREAM3_BATCH_CANDIDATES * GLOBAL_SPILL_SCALE_PPM`.
- Kaggle version 32 compiled but stopped on explicit Stream3 spill-capacity fatal at depth 5 (`spill_end=1584778`, `spill_capacity=1572864`). Default spill derivation now includes the Stream4 worker multiplier: `STREAM4_ACTIVE_SORT_SLOTS * STREAM4_ACTIVE_SORT_SLOTS * STREAM3_BATCH_CANDIDATES * GLOBAL_SPILL_SCALE_PPM`.
- Added per-depth stream timing diagnostics. `depth_done` now logs Stream1/2 ring graph time, Stream3 ring time, Stream3 final spill-drain time, final filter/materialize/reset time, Stream4 job time/avg/max, Stream5 threshold time, Stream4 queue peaks, and global spill peak.
- Added master debug gating. `BEAM_ENABLE_DEBUG=OFF` now forces depth logs, general debug logs, stream timing, inference trace, and path trace off at compile time. Subflags are `BEAM_DEBUG_STREAM_TIMING`, `BEAM_DEBUG_INFERENCE_TRACE`, and `BEAM_DEBUG_PATH_TRACE`; Kaggle diagnostic config now enables stream timing only.
- Added debug-only final request validation before final materialization. New master-gated subflag `BEAM_DEBUG_FINAL_VALIDATE` pre-fills the final request buffer with invalid sentinel values before final scatter, validates every `FinalRequest` for `parent_idx < current_frontier_size`, `target_local_idx < final_request_count`, `target_local_idx == request_slot`, and `move < MOVE_COUNT`, then throws a fatal diagnostic with the first invalid request before `final_materialize_cuda` can issue an illegal memory access.
- Moved runtime config auto-detection out of `tools/production_runner.cu` into `cuda/runtime_config.hpp` and `cuda/runtime_config.cpp`. The config module now owns memory-budget search, `STREAM3_RING_SLOTS` auto-selection, `SHARD_COUNT`/`STREAM4_BATCH_CANDIDATES` selection, and job/work estimates.
- Changed config-search scoring to estimate gross generated flow (`N_LOCAL * MOVE_COUNT`) rather than final frontier size only. Stream3 job estimates now use gross generated candidates, and Stream4 job estimates use configurable `BEAM_STREAM4_FLOW_SCALE_PPM` to account for Stream3/Stream4 post-dedup flow.
- Added local Docker debug image `gpu-dev-cutlass-nsight:2026-05-24` from the existing `gpu-dev:latest` base. The image now includes local CUTLASS under `/opt/cutlass` and Nsight Systems symlinks in `/usr/local/bin`; `docker-compose.yml` uses the new image for `beam-tests` and `nsight-profile`.
- Changed local Docker run practice so `docker exec` output is mirrored to Docker Desktop container logs by piping command output through `/proc/1/fd/1` while also saving logs under `test_results/`.
- Made static memory sizing mirror allocator alignment with `LayoutSizeCursor` for stream, final, and solved layouts. This prevents small-layout underallocation and makes byte estimates match actual pointer placement.
- Fixed CUDA tests after the owner/shard distribution mixer change: Stream3 test fixtures now use hashes that map to the expected owner/shard, and the threshold test copies only the allocated final frontier capacity instead of the full survivor shard buffer.
- Added `/build-debug-docker/` to `.gitignore` because the local Docker debug container creates this generated CMake/Ninja build tree through the bind mount.
- Docker CUDA verification after the local image/log/layout/test fixes: build passed; `ctest --test-dir build-debug-docker --output-on-failure` passed 12/12; `production_runner 0 1 4194304` smoke passed with visible Docker Desktop logs and saved logs under `test_results/`.
- Added manual runtime config mode. `BEAM_RUNTIME_CONFIG_MODE=manual` or `BEAM_MANUAL_CONFIG=1` bypasses config search and requires explicit `BEAM_STREAM3_RING_SLOTS`, `BEAM_SHARD_COUNT`, `BEAM_STREAM4_BATCH_CANDIDATES`, `BEAM_SHARD_CAPACITY_CANDIDATES`, and `BEAM_GLOBAL_SPILL_CAPACITY`; `BEAM_RING_COUNT` is optional and otherwise derived from logical shard size.
- Added `throughput_probe` per-depth log line under stream timing debug. The probe reports Stream3 average batch time, Stream3 candidate throughput, Stream4 average batch time, Stream4 candidate throughput, spill peak, spill capacity, and spill peak ratio.
- Manual local benchmark with `beam=4194304`, `depth_limit=9`, `SHARD_COUNT=6`, `STREAM4_BATCH_CANDIDATES=699392`, `SHARD_CAPACITY_CANDIDATES=874496`, and `GLOBAL_SPILL_CAPACITY=6291456` still failed at depth 5 from explicit Stream3 spill guard (`spill_end=6294467`, `spill_capacity=6291456`), while throughput probe lines were emitted. Report saved as `test_results/manual_config_throughput_probe_2026-05-24.md`.
- Added experimental per-logical-shard double-buffer resident storage. `BEAM_SHARD_BUFFER_COUNT=2` allocates `survivor_shard_A/B` per logical shard, maps Stream3 writes through `stream3_write_buffer_index`, disables default global spill capacity, and runs Stream4 over physical storage shards.
- Added double-buffer Stream3 safety behavior: Stream3 prepare now selects a writable non-processing physical shard buffer at collect time; dispatcher backpressure blocks Stream3 when any logical shard lacks reserved write capacity, queues ready clean/dirty buffers to Stream4, and fails explicitly with fatal code `3002` if no buffer can accept the group.
- Verified double-buffer/no-global-spill local Docker path with manual config `beam=1048576`, `depth_limit=9`, `SHARD_COUNT=4`, `SHARD_BUFFER_COUNT=2`, `STREAM4_BATCH_CANDIDATES=196608`, `SHARD_CAPACITY_CANDIDATES=262144`, `STREAM4_ACTIVE_SORT_SLOTS=2`, `GLOBAL_SPILL_CAPACITY=0`. Run completed in `5.88652s`; depths 5-8 were `1.39534s`, `1.41985s`, `1.3442s`, `1.32157s`; `global_spill_peak=0`. Log saved as `test_results/docker_double_buffer_smoke_backpressure_queue_p0_d9_b1048576_s4_buf2_2026-05-24.log`.
- Tested double-buffer/no-global-spill mode at `beam=8388608`, `depth_limit=9`, `SHARD_COUNT=4`, `SHARD_BUFFER_COUNT=2`, `STREAM4_BATCH_CANDIDATES=196608`, `SHARD_CAPACITY_CANDIDATES=2097152`, `STREAM4_ACTIVE_SORT_SLOTS=2`, `GLOBAL_SPILL_CAPACITY=0`. Run completed without spill or crash in `188.916s`; depths 6-8 were `53.1781s`, `55.2412s`, `69.0317s`; Stream4 average batch time degraded to `25.08ms`, `26.09ms`, `32.76ms`. Log saved as `test_results/docker_double_buffer_p0_d9_b8388608_s4_buf2_cap2097152_2026-05-24.log`.
- Added `STREAM4_TRIGGER_CANDIDATES` as a separate Stream4 launch threshold. `STREAM4_BATCH_CANDIDATES` remains a Stream4 job-size/config-search parameter; `stream3_build_ready_shard_queue_cuda` now launches dirty shard buffers only on forced flush, `dirty_count >= STREAM4_TRIGGER_CANDIDATES`, or near-capacity backpressure.
- Verified manual double-buffer trigger run in Docker with `beam=8388608`, `depth_limit=9`, `SHARD_COUNT=4`, `SHARD_BUFFER_COUNT=2`, `STREAM4_BATCH_CANDIDATES=196608`, `STREAM4_TRIGGER_CANDIDATES=786432`, `SHARD_CAPACITY_CANDIDATES=2097152`, `STREAM4_ACTIVE_SORT_SLOTS=2`, `GLOBAL_SPILL_CAPACITY=0`. Full-beam depths 6-8 ran in `9.35333s`, `9.03494s`, and `9.23598s`; `stream4_jobs` dropped to `285/283/288`; `global_spill_peak=0`. Log saved as `test_results/docker_stream4_trigger_p0_d9_b8388608_trigger786432_2026-05-24.log`.
- Ran local Docker `beam=16777216`, `depth_limit=60`, depth-logs-only build, manual config `SHARD_COUNT=4`, `SHARD_BUFFER_COUNT=2`, `STREAM4_BATCH_CANDIDATES=196608`, `STREAM4_TRIGGER_CANDIDATES=786432`, `SHARD_CAPACITY_CANDIDATES=4194304`, `STREAM4_ACTIVE_SORT_SLOTS=2`, `GLOBAL_SPILL_CAPACITY=0`. Puzzle 0 solved at `depth=54` with `solution_length=55`, `elapsed_sec=1441.43`, `run_exit_code=0`. Full-beam depths mostly ran around `28-31s`, with one outlier `depth35=80.925s`. Log saved as `test_results/docker_depth60_p0_b16777216_depthlogs_only_2026-05-24.log`.
- Published the working solver flow. GitHub `main` was updated to commit `4728a23` (`Add double-buffer Stream4 trigger runtime`). Kaggle kernel `trydotatwo/cayley-beam-gpu-runner` was pushed as public version 37 with notebook defaults `beam=2**24`, `depth_limit=60`, manual double-buffer config, and no tracked solution path injection. Kaggle API reported `KernelWorkerStatus.RUNNING` immediately after push.

## 2026-05-26
- Restored the local repository baseline to commit `6d0ab88` before the new multi-GPU work, matching the user requirement to remove earlier same-day experimental changes.
- Added the first C++/CUDA multi-rank execution path without a new branch: `production_runner` reads `WORLD_SIZE`, `RANK`, and `LOCAL_RANK`, calls `cudaSetDevice(LOCAL_RANK)`, creates an NCCL communicator through file rendezvous, and passes the communicator into dispatcher code.
- Extended Stream 5 memory planning with segmented send/receive slots. `stream5_slot_count` equals `ring_count` for multi-rank and `1` for single-rank. `BEAM_STREAM5_RECV_CAPACITY_SCALE_PPM` controls receive slot headroom; default is `2000000`.
- Wired Stream 3 multi-rank candidate flow: each ring writes remote `CandidateMeta` into its own Stream 5 send slot, Stream 5 exchanges counts and payloads via NCCL, and received remote candidates are collected into local shard buffers before Stream 4 processing.
- Added multi-rank finalization primitives: NCCL global score histogram, exact less/equal threshold split, target-rank load balancing, final history metadata exchange, `FinalRequest` exchange, `FinalResponse` exchange, and scatter into the next local frontier.
- Added rank-aware solution reconstruction without forced disk history: on global stop, all ranks finish their local CPU history, NCCL gathers solved metadata, the solved/controller rank broadcasts small parent-chain queries, source ranks read local RAM/disk history entries, and NCCL broadcasts responses until the full move path is reconstructed.
- Kept the `WORLD_SIZE=1` path as the existing single-GPU path: multi-rank collectives and extra Stream 5 exchange loops are only active when `world_size > 1`.
- Updated the Kaggle notebook to launch one C++ process per rank and set `WORLD_SIZE`, `RANK`, `LOCAL_RANK`, and `BEAM_NCCL_ID_FILE`. The notebook also supports a source-archive dataset so Kaggle can test uncommitted local work without requiring a GitHub push.
- Local verification passed after rank-aware reconstruction: `git diff --check`, Docker build target `production_runner`, targeted CUDA tests (`stream5`, `final`, `threshold`, `dispatcher`, `static_memory`) with 5/5 passed, and `WORLD_SIZE=1` production smoke `production_runner 0 1 4194304`.
- Local machine exposes only one CUDA GPU (`NVIDIA GeForce RTX 3070 Laptop GPU`), so local `WORLD_SIZE=2` execution was not possible.
- Kaggle 2xT4 execution is pending explicit approval to make the current uncommitted source available to Kaggle. The safer source-archive dataset upload was blocked by approval policy because it would export the current workspace to an external Kaggle dataset.
- Pushed `main` to GitHub commit `2ddabc4` (`Add multi-rank Stream5 flow`) and launched Kaggle kernel version 48 on T4 x2. Kaggle build completed, but notebook output stayed silent during C++ execution because rank stdout was redirected to per-rank files and printed only after both rank processes exited.
- Added Kaggle notebook config `LIVE_LOG_RANKS`, supporting `"all"`, `"none"`, or a rank list like `[0, 1]`. Rank processes now stream stdout through Python pipes, write every line immediately to `run_logs/puzzle_*_rank*.log`, and live-print selected ranks to Kaggle UI without duplicate post-run printing.
- Kaggle version 49 with live rank logs reached multi-rank depth 1 and failed on rank 1 with `final request return rank exceeds WORLD_SIZE`. Root cause: multi-rank final `exchange_u64_items` accepted send-count/send-offset vectors by reference while the same caller reused `recv_offsets` as the send-offset vector for the final-request exchange; inside `exchange_u64_items`, `make_offsets(recv_counts, recv_offsets)` mutated the send offsets before payload exchange. Fixed by snapshotting send counts and offsets at function entry before receiving peer counts.
- Verification after the final-exchange offset fix: Docker build target `production_runner` passed, targeted CUDA tests passed 5/5, and `WORLD_SIZE=1` smoke `production_runner 0 1 4194304` passed.
- Kaggle version 50 still failed at multi-rank depth 1 on rank 1 with `final request return rank exceeds WORLD_SIZE`, so the send-offset snapshot fix was not sufficient.
- Added master-gated final exchange trace logging behind `BEAM_DEBUG_FINAL_EXCHANGE_TRACE`. The trace is compiled only when `BEAM_ENABLE_DEBUG=ON` and the subflag is enabled; production builds keep the trace compiled out. The trace logs multi-rank final threshold counts, selected candidate samples, history/request/response exchange counts, first/last request samples, and the invalid request payload before throwing.
- Changed the Kaggle notebook diagnostic config to enable full rank debug logs and `BEAM_DEBUG_FINAL_EXCHANGE_TRACE=ON` for the next 2xT4 run. This is diagnostic-only; no algorithmic final-exchange behavior was changed.
- Local verification after adding final-exchange trace logging: `git diff --check` passed, Kaggle notebook JSON parsed, Docker CUDA targeted tests passed 5/5, and `WORLD_SIZE=1` smoke `production_runner 0 1 4194304` passed.
- Kaggle version 51 trace confirmed the remaining multi-rank final failure cause: the final request exchange reused `recv_offsets` for `request` send offsets, then the preceding `history` exchange overwrote `recv_offsets` with history receive offsets before `request` exchange entry. Evidence: rank 1 depth 1 logged `request_send_counts offset1=97 offset2=209`, then `request_send_snapshot offset1=0 offset2=204`, matching `history_recv` offsets and causing corrupted `FinalRequest` payloads.
- Fixed the final exchange offset lifetime bug by adding immutable host-side `FinalExchangePlan{count, offset, total}` objects per exchange phase. `history`, `request`, and `response` now keep independent send plans, and `exchange_u64_items` builds a local receive plan per call instead of reusing caller scratch offsets.
- Tightened final exchange alignment: `FinalHistoryRecord` is now `alignas(32)` and exactly 64 bytes, `next_frontier_states_tmp` and `final_response_buffer` are allocated with at least `CandidateMeta` alignment because multi-rank final overlays them with `CandidateMeta`/`FinalHistoryRecord`, and final count/offset device service buffers are 256-byte aligned.
- Local verification after the offset-plan/alignment fix: Docker build target `production_runner` passed, targeted CUDA tests passed 5/5, and `WORLD_SIZE=1` smoke `production_runner 0 1 4194304` passed.
- Documented the three scratch-pool overlay layouts: Stream inference layout, final select/dedup layout, and final materialization layout. Layout 3 owns the static 3-slot ring for chunked GPU-GPU materialization and defines the final scratch sizing floor.
- Reworked multi-rank final materialization to chunk selected `CandidateMeta` on GPU. Requests are built from selected chunks on GPU, grouped by `source_rank`, exchanged through NCCL without CPU payload staging, materialized by source rank, regrouped by `return_rank`, and scattered into the local `next_frontier_states_tmp`. Self-source and self-return paths stay local and skip NCCL payload traffic.
- Added static layout-3 buffers: selected candidate storage, rank key ping-pong arrays, request ping-pong arrays, request receive arrays, response send/receive arrays, history send/receive arrays, and fixed CUB temp. `FinalHistoryRecord` is now a shared 64-byte/32-aligned project type.
- Replaced final exchange host control vectors in the new multi-rank final materialization path with fixed-size static arrays (`kFinalExchangeMaxWorldSize=128`) so per-chunk final request/response planning does not allocate dynamic host containers.
- Moved debug final request validation error storage from `cudaMalloc` to a static `layout_final` slot (`FinalRequestValidationError`, 48 bytes, 16-byte aligned), removing the last CUDA dynamic allocation inside final materialization code.
- Extended `static_memory_cuda_tests` with multi-rank layout-3 capacity/alignment assertions for 3 slots, exchange capacity, selected-buffer sizing, request/response/history slot pointers, and 256-byte CUB temp alignment.
- Local verification after layout-3 materialization changes: Docker full build passed, targeted CUDA tests passed 4/4 (`final_cuda_tests`, `threshold_cuda_tests`, `dispatcher_cuda_tests`, `static_memory_cuda_tests`), and a manual small-config `WORLD_SIZE=1` `production_runner 0 1 4096` smoke passed. Local default auto-config smoke still fails on the RTX 3070 Laptop GPU budget, so real multi-rank runtime validation remains pending on Kaggle T4x2. Report saved as `test_results/final_materialize_layout3_2026-05-26.md`.
- Reduced multi-rank final materialization static memory: full `final_request_buffer[N_LOCAL]` is now allocated only for `WORLD_SIZE=1`, full `final_response_buffer` is omitted for `WORLD_SIZE>1`, and the multi-rank selected-candidate buffer is capped at `min(GLOBAL_BEAM_WIDTH_EFFECTIVE, survivor_count)` instead of using the whole `State128` frontier byte area.
- Added layout-3 slot events for multi-rank final materialization: `build_done`, `history_done`, `request_done`, `response_ready`, and `response_done`. Final request/history/response exchanges now record payload completion events, and response receive scatter is deferred until slot drain so response NCCL transfer can overlap later chunk work.
- Kept the final exchange receive-offset host-to-device copy synchronous before payload enqueue so async payload execution never references stack-backed receive offset arrays after the exchange helper returns.
- Updated `static_memory_cuda_tests` expectations for the reduced multi-rank layout: full request/response buffers must be absent in multi-rank final materialization, while 3-slot request/response/history buffers and fixed CUB temp remain statically allocated and aligned.
- Prepared the Kaggle T4x2 validation path to use GitHub `main` as the source of truth rather than a source-archive dataset. The notebook diagnostic depth is set to 8 for a short multi-rank final-materialization test.
- Diagnosed the next Kaggle T4x2 failure from user-provided logs: final materialization progressed past the previous exchange corruption, then rank 1 failed during depth 6 `stream3_remote_recv_collect` with fatal code `3002` (`STREAM_FATAL_STREAM3_DOUBLE_BUFFER_OVERFLOW`). No code was changed during this diagnostic pass. The observed state was physical shard 7 processing with `clean=3988985`, `dirty=146907`, `existing=4135892`, `available=0`, `raw_count=24057`, and `GLOBAL_SPILL_CAPACITY=0`; likely design gaps are missing remote-receive backpressure and disabled synchronized periodic threshold updates for multi-rank depth flow.
- Added diagnostic-only fatal trace fields for Stream3 double-buffer overflow: logical shard, selected buffer, sibling physical shard, sibling clean/dirty/processing counts, sibling existing/available capacity, and shard buffer count. No scheduling or backpressure behavior changed.
- Enabled all existing Kaggle diagnostic logging switches for the next T4x2 run: Stream timing, inference trace, path trace, final validation, final exchange trace, depth logs, debug logs, live rank logs, and runtime `BEAM_DEBUG_PIPELINE_STATS`.
- Kaggle version 54 reproduced Stream3 fatal code `3002` with expanded diagnostics: both ranks failed in `stream3_remote_recv_collect` at depth 6 after both physical buffers for the target logical shard became `processing=1`. Rank 0 failed on logical shard 2 (`selected=4`, sibling `5`) and rank 1 failed on logical shard 3 (`selected=7`, sibling `6`). Current leading hypothesis: Stream4 can start processing a physical shard that Stream3 previously counted as the writable buffer while a Stream3 ring is still in flight; the local writable-buffer check is not a reservation, and remote receive has no independent ingress reserve. Report saved as `test_results/kaggle_v54_stream3_diagnostics_2026-05-26.md`.
- Fixed the Stream4 ready-queue A/B scheduling invariant. `stream3_build_ready_shard_queue_kernel` now builds readiness per logical shard, skips any logical shard with an active Stream4 physical buffer, emits at most one physical buffer per logical shard, prioritizes dirty-ready buffers over clean-only buffers during forced flush, and updates `stream3_write_buffer_index` to a non-processing sibling when one has capacity. Added Stream3 CUDA regression tests for logical-shard mutual exclusion, busy-sibling skip, and dirty-priority final flush. Updated `ARCHITECTURE_NEED.md` with the logical/physical shard contract: Stream3 is the only Stream4 buffer writer, Stream5 only fills `remote_recv_buffer`, and Stream4 may process at most one A/B physical buffer per logical shard at a time.
- Disabled Stream3 pre-launch writable-buffer backpressure for multi-rank execution. The dispatcher now keeps the backpressure ready-queue drain only when `WORLD_SIZE == 1`; when `WORLD_SIZE > 1`, Stream3 launches normally and any lack of A/B write capacity remains an explicit fatal overflow instead of being hidden by scheduler throttling. Updated `ARCHITECTURE_NEED.md` with this single-GPU-only backpressure rule.
- Kaggle version 55 confirmed the A/B logical-shard mutex fix changed the failure mode: neither rank had both A/B buffers processing for the failed logical shard. Rank 1 failed with selected physical shard 0 non-processing but nearly full (`existing=4176369`, `available=17935`, `raw_count=24033`) while sibling physical shard 1 was processing; rank 0 failed similarly on logical shard 3. The revealed issue is multi-rank capacity saturation because periodic threshold updates were disabled for `WORLD_SIZE>1`, causing per-rank clean totals around 31M before depth 6 drained.
- Enabled periodic threshold updates for multi-rank without cross-rank synchronization. Periodic multi-rank updates now build a local completed-shard histogram and update `current_threshold` from that local histogram only; final global threshold still uses NCCL/global aggregation after local final flush.
- Softened the remaining single-GPU double-buffer backpressure path: if the writable-buffer reserve check fails but no ready Stream4 work exists, the dispatcher logs `stream3_backpressure_bypass` and launches Stream3 instead of throwing a scheduler error. Multi-rank execution still bypasses this pre-launch backpressure entirely.
- Kaggle version 57 diagnostic run on T4x2 failed with `code=3002` after depth 5. Stream timings show depth 5 wall time about `2.95s`, Stream4 cumulative time about `3.12-3.18s` across two slots, Stream3 ring time about `1.94s`, Stream1/2 time about `1.13s`, and Stream5 time about `0.38s`. Buffer diagnostics show both ranks near `32M` clean resident candidates and `threshold_updates_depth=0` before fatal. Failure occurs during `stream3_remote_recv_collect` before the scheduler gets a chance to update threshold, so the current issue is threshold scheduling/pruning timing rather than the old A/B mutex bug. Report saved as `test_results/kaggle_v57_stream_timing_buffers_2026-05-26.md`.
- Replaced the previous multi-rank local-only periodic threshold update with a Stream5-owned collective request/update path. Multi-rank dispatch now reflects completed local Stream4 work into `threshold_request_local`, all ranks run an `ncclAllReduce(Max)` request check at Stream5 exchange points, and any rank request triggers a global histogram allreduce plus monotonic threshold update on all ranks before remote receive collection. The dispatcher refreshes completed Stream4 slots immediately before the Stream5 request check so jobs that finish while waiting for Stream3 are counted. Added double-buffered `current_threshold[2]`, `threshold_initialized[2]`, and `current_threshold_active_index`; Stream5 writes the inactive slot and commits by active-index update after a device fence, while Stream3/Stream4 read only the committed slot. Added service fields `threshold_request_local/global`; no new atomics were introduced. Removed debug-only atomics from final request validation, leaving atomics only in Stream2. Docker verification: release `production_runner` build passed; full diagnostic `production_runner` build with `BEAM_DEBUG_FINAL_VALIDATE=ON` passed; `threshold_cuda_tests`, `static_memory_cuda_tests`, and `dispatcher_cuda_tests` passed. Local `production_runner 0 8 1048576` and `0 8 262144` could not run on the current GPU because runtime config fitting failed with `budget=6658457600`, `free_before=7463763968`, `headroom=805306368`. Verification note saved as `test_results/threshold_stream5_collective_verification_2026-05-26.md`.
- Pushed commit `884b042` to GitHub `main` and launched Kaggle kernel version 58 for T4x2 validation. Kaggle status ended as `KernelWorkerStatus.CANCEL_ACKNOWLEDGED`. Downloaded logs contain no `code=3002`, `cuda stream fatal`, `terminate`, or `what()` matches. Both ranks completed depth 4 and entered depth 5; the captured tail reached depth-5 final spill drain and Stream4 drain with `global_spill=0`, but no `depth_done=5` or final-run completion line was captured before cancellation. Runtime logs are stored under `test_results/kaggle_v58_output/`.
- Added detailed `threshold_trace` diagnostics behind `BEAM_DEBUG_FINAL_EXCHANGE_TRACE`. The dispatcher now logs Stream5 threshold request begin/done, final threshold begin/done, and every stage of `update_threshold_global`: local histogram begin/enqueued/done, local-to-global copy or NCCL histogram allreduce begin/enqueued/done, threshold publish begin/enqueued/done, active threshold slot, threshold value, and initialized flags. In this diagnostic build only, the trace adds stream synchronizations after local histogram, NCCL histogram, and threshold publish so a silent stall can be localized to the exact stage. Verification passed: `git diff --check`, Docker release `production_runner` build, Docker diagnostic `production_runner` build, and targeted CUDA tests (`threshold_cuda_tests`, `dispatcher_cuda_tests`, `static_memory_cuda_tests`) 3/3.
- Diagnosed Kaggle version 59 T4x2 stall from downloaded logs under `test_results/kaggle_v59_output/`: no explicit fatal error was emitted; rank 1 entered `stream5_collective_periodic` and reached `nccl_histogram_allreduce_enqueued` at `137.257s` after `force_local_request=1`, while rank 0 skipped the matching boundary threshold request and entered `final_spill_drain` with `stream3_jobs=1024`, `stream4_jobs=112`, and `dirty_total=2507064`. Fixed the collective ordering bug by making every multi-rank dispatcher enter the end-of-depth threshold request collective, using `local_request = (stream4_jobs_since_threshold_update != 0)` so any rank can request the threshold update without desynchronizing NCCL call order. Verification passed: `git diff --check`, Docker release `production_runner` build, Docker diagnostic `production_runner` build, and targeted CUDA tests (`threshold_cuda_tests`, `dispatcher_cuda_tests`, `static_memory_cuda_tests`) 3/3.
- Kaggle version 60 T4x2 progressed past the previous boundary threshold collective stall and failed later in final selection. Both ranks completed the forced boundary threshold update before final spill drain; rank 1 then threw `final selected count does not match score phase counts` after final threshold selection. Evidence: `final_threshold=24669`, `global_less=18148438`, `global_equal=25974`, `global_keep_count=16777216`; `global_less > global_keep_count` means the host-side final selected-count expectation does not match the capped exact-selection path. Logs are stored under `test_results/kaggle_v60_output/`; diagnostic note saved as `test_results/kaggle_v60_final_selected_count_2026-05-26.md`. No code or architecture changes were made after the user forbade unapproved code changes.
- Added separate compile-time debug flag `BEAM_DEBUG_FINAL_HISTOGRAM_TRACE`. When `BEAM_ENABLE_DEBUG=ON`, the flag logs `final_histogram_trace` at multi-rank final selection: global/local histogram prefix counts, exact less/equal counts from `survivor_shard + clean_count`, invariant status, deltas, and per-shard active/inactive histogram totals versus `clean_count`. Kaggle diagnostic notebook enables the flag. No final-selection algorithm behavior changed.
- Added separate compile-time debug flag `BEAM_DEBUG_STREAM4_HISTOGRAM_TRACE`. When `BEAM_ENABLE_DEBUG=ON`, the dispatcher logs `stream4_histogram_trace` before Stream4 graph launch and after Stream4 slot completion: rank, physical/logical shard, slot, active/inactive histogram index, clean/dirty counts, current threshold slot/value, A/B totals, active/inactive less/equal counts, deltas versus `clean_count`, scratch count, queue state, and Stream3/Stream4 job counters. Kaggle diagnostic notebooks enable the flag. No Stream4 algorithm behavior changed.
- Verification after `BEAM_DEBUG_STREAM4_HISTOGRAM_TRACE`: `git diff --check` passed, both Kaggle notebooks parsed as JSON, Docker debug `production_runner` build passed with `BEAM_DEBUG_STREAM4_HISTOGRAM_TRACE=ON`, and targeted CUDA tests passed 3/3 (`threshold_cuda_tests`, `dispatcher_cuda_tests`, `static_memory_cuda_tests`).
- Added Kaggle diagnostic runner auto-stop config `RUN_TIMEOUT_SEC=200`. The timeout applies only to spawned `production_runner` rank processes after setup/build, terminates live ranks after 200 seconds, kills remaining ranks after a 10-second grace period, and returns `return_code=-200` while preserving rank logs.
- Fixed the host Stream4 scheduler invariant after Kaggle v71 showed duplicate launches for one physical shard. The dispatcher now consumes `stream3_ready_count` after host append, dedupes pending work by physical shard, tracks running work by logical shard, scans the pending queue for launchable shards so other logical shards are not blocked by a busy A/B sibling, and enforces at most one running Stream4 job per logical shard. A physical sibling selected while the other A/B buffer is still running remains pending with `processing_flag=1` rather than being launched concurrently or dropped. Single-GPU backpressure now uses a worst-case `STREAM3_BATCH_CANDIDATES` reserve when a logical shard has a processing sibling. Final flush now allows more rounds for sequential A/B cleanup.
- Verification after the Stream4 scheduler invariant fix: Docker `production_runner` rebuild passed, targeted CUDA tests passed 3/3 (`threshold_cuda_tests`, `dispatcher_cuda_tests`, `static_memory_cuda_tests`), and a local 1xGPU manual smoke `production_runner 0 8 1048576` with `SHARD_COUNT=4`, `SHARD_BUFFER_COUNT=2`, `STREAM4_BATCH_CANDIDATES=196608`, `STREAM4_TRIGGER_CANDIDATES=196608`, `SHARD_CAPACITY_CANDIDATES=262144`, `STREAM4_ACTIVE_SORT_SLOTS=2`, `GLOBAL_SPILL_CAPACITY=0` passed in `21.1695s`.
- Kaggle version 72 validated the Stream4 scheduler fix on T4x2. The run completed depth 8 with `return_code=0` in `37.132s`; depth 7 had `stream4_jobs=64`, `stream4_busy_max=2`, `global_spill_peak=0`, and `threshold_updates_depth=9` on both ranks. Log scan found `stream4_complete_post active_matches_clean=0` count `0`, so the v71 concurrent A/B Stream4 histogram corruption was not reproduced. The remaining `hist_invariant_ok=0` entries occur only at early non-full-beam depths where stale active histograms remain in physical buffers with `clean_count=0`; full-beam depths 5-7 have `hist_invariant_ok=1`.
- Prepared the next Kaggle T4x2 validation run for depth 16. The diagnostic notebooks now set `DEPTH_LIMIT=16` and `RUN_TIMEOUT_SEC=600` so the spawned `production_runner` ranks have enough time to reach the deeper validation target while still preserving an auto-stop bound.
- Kaggle version 73 validated the Stream4 scheduler fix through depth 16 on T4x2. The run completed with `return_code=0`, rank seconds around `158.49`, `completed_depths=16`, `last_final_frontier_size=8388608`, `last_final_threshold=20139`, and `threshold_updates=84`. Log scans found no fatal patterns, no `stream4_complete_post active_matches_clean=0`, no `global_spill_peak>0`, and `hist_invariant_ok=1` for full-beam depths 5-15. Report saved as `test_results/kaggle_v73_depth16_validation_2026-05-27.md`.
- Fixed the Kaggle manual T4x2 config for `beam=2**26`: `SHARD_CAPACITY_CANDIDATES` is now derived from `LOCAL_BEAM_WIDTH = ceil(BEAM_WIDTH / WORLD_SIZE)` and `LOGICAL_SHARD_SIZE = ceil(LOCAL_BEAM_WIDTH / SHARD_COUNT)`, then scaled by `SHARD_CAPACITY_SCALE_PPM=1250000` and aligned to `STREAM4_BATCH_ALIGNMENT=1024`. This replaces the previous incorrect global-beam formula `BEAM_WIDTH // SHARD_COUNT`, which over-allocated per-rank shard capacity by `WORLD_SIZE`.
- Reworked static scratch memory planning into explicit phase layouts. `StaticMemoryPlan` now reports `layout_phase1_streams_bytes`, `layout_phase2_select_bytes`, and `layout_phase3_materialize_bytes`; `scratch_pool_bytes` is the max of those three layouts. Final selection outputs that are consumed by materialization remain in a common prefix, while phase2 filter temp buffers overlay phase3 materialization temp buffers after that prefix.
- Updated runtime config search to stop requiring `layout_streams_bytes <= layout_final_budget_bytes`; Stream layouts and final layouts are separate phase layouts over one scratch pool, so candidate acceptance now relies on total static memory budget. Added validation that Stream4 batch/trigger sizes do not exceed physical shard capacity.
- Enforced the resident A/B shard contract in runtime config. `RuntimeConfig::shard_buffer_count` now defaults to `2`, auto and manual config reject any value other than `2`, and `ARCHITECTURE_NEED.md` records that every logical shard always has two resident physical shard buffers. The legacy single-buffer global spill path is now unreachable under the supported config contract.
- Verification after final phase-layout repacking and forced A/B config: `git diff --check` passed, Docker CUDA build target `static_memory_cuda_tests production_runner` passed, `static_memory_cuda_tests` passed, and manual A/B single-GPU smoke `production_runner 0 8 1048576` passed with `SHARD_BUFFER_COUNT=2` and `GLOBAL_SPILL_CAPACITY=0`. Report saved as `test_results/static_final_phase_layout_2026-05-27.md`.
- Kaggle version 75 tested `BEAM_WIDTH=2**26` on T4x2 with forced resident A/B buffers. The run passed memory budgeting and reached final selection, then failed with `final selected count does not match score phase counts` after `40.029s`. Evidence: `final_threshold=27609`, `global_keep_count=67108864`, histogram counts `hist_less=67087422`, `hist_equal=36345`, exact survivor scan `exact_global_less=67216299`, `exact_global_equal=31755`, `diff_global_less=+128877`, `hist_invariant_ok=1`, and `exact_invariant_ok=0`. This points to a histogram-vs-exact score-key mismatch, not a capacity or Stream3 A/B overflow failure. Report saved as `test_results/kaggle_v75_beam2p26_failure_2026-05-27.md`.
- Extended `BEAM_DEBUG_FINAL_HISTOGRAM_TRACE` without changing runtime architecture. The final debug path now copies `survivor_shard` one physical shard at a time on the host, builds an exact local score histogram, logs exact-vs-Stream4 histogram totals per shard, logs threshold-window bins around `final_threshold`, and logs the top 32 local bin differences. No new kernels, atomics, runtime buffers, scheduling changes, or threshold algorithm changes were added.
- Updated `dispatcher_cuda_tests` to expect Stream4 graph templates per resident physical shard (`plan.storage_shard_count`) rather than per logical shard (`config.shard_count`). This aligns the test with the forced A/B runtime contract and does not change runtime behavior.
- Kaggle version 76 ran the new exact final histogram diagnostics on T4x2 with `BEAM_WIDTH=2**26`. The run reproduced `final selected count does not match score phase counts` after `40.021s`. New evidence points to final phase scratch overlay corrupting `survivor_shard`: rank 0 reported `score=0 local_hist=0 exact_host=4886157`, rank 1 reported `score=0 local_hist=0 exact_host=4323292`, while Stream4 active histogram totals still matched `clean_count`. The likely cause is `final_keep_flags` or another phase-2 final buffer overlapping live `CandidateMeta` survivor storage before selected candidates are materialized. Report saved as `test_results/kaggle_v76_final_histogram_exact_debug_2026-05-27.md`.
- Fixed the phase-2 scratch overlay lifetime bug without changing final algorithms. `layout_phase2_select` still starts at the scratch-pool base, but phase-1 live inputs needed by final selection (`survivor_shard`, `clean_count`, committed shard histograms, local/global score histograms, threshold state, threshold request fields) are now placed after `layout_phase2_select_bytes`. Dead Stream3/Stream4/Stream5 scratch remains allowed to overlay phase-2 final buffers. `layout_phase3_materialize` still overlays phase-2 temporary filter buffers after the common selected-candidate prefix, so phase-3 may reuse the old phase-1 live-input memory after selection has consumed it. Added `static_memory_cuda_tests` range assertions that the whole phase-2 select layout does not overlap live phase-1 inputs.

Static memory stayed within T4 budget: `scratch_pool_bytes=8005674752`, `layout_phase1_streams_bytes=7338354432`, `layout_phase2_select_bytes=2485650688`, `layout_phase3_materialize_bytes=8005674752`, `static_allocation_bytes=12300679168`, `gpu_budget_bytes=14640021504`. - Added `BEAM_HISTORY_MODE=static_hybrid` for CPU candidate history storage. The runner now keeps `HISTORY_SLOT_COUNT` pinned `CandidateMeta[32B]` copy slots, counts those slots against `BEAM_HISTORY_RAM_BYTES`, preallocates a bounded RAM `HistoryEntry[16B]` arena, pre-sizes a single per-rank disk arena file from `BEAM_HISTORY_DISK_BYTES`, writes new history to disk first, and falls back to RAM if the configured disk arena is exhausted or a disk write fails. No pruning/remapping, GPU stream scheduling, finalization, or distributed reconstruction semantics changed; `route_packed.source_rank + parent_idx` remain the only rank/index linkage. Kaggle notebooks now use `HISTORY_MODE=static_hybrid`, `HISTORY_SLOT_COUNT=2`, `HISTORY_RAM_BYTES=28*1000**3`, `HISTORY_DISK_BYTES=49*1000**3`, and `/tmp/beam_history_arena` so sparse history arena files are not packaged as notebook outputs.

Rank 0 depth 9 reported `history_bytes_stored=2346674752`, `history_bytes_stored_ram=0`, and `history_bytes_stored_disk=1809803840`, confirming disk-first history storage was active. - Added `BEAM_SOLVED_NEIGHBORHOOD_RADIUS` for the first Stream2 solved-neighborhood implementation. Radius `0` preserves the old exact `central_state` comparison. Radius `>0` builds an inverse-move CPU neighborhood before depth processing, stores `Hash128 -> packed suffix` on CPU, publishes a read-only GPU table of `fingerprint_slots[bucket][4]` and `hash_slots[bucket][4]`, and lets Stream2 check two fixed buckets by fingerprint with full `Hash128` confirmation. CPU appends the suffix after history prefix reconstruction. Local and multi-rank solved-hit selection now compare prefix depth plus suffix length before reconstruction. No K2 descendant expansion was implemented.
- Documented future K2 separately: Stream2 may later expand generated-candidate descendants by a separate radius with precomputed suffix generators; K2 suffix generators must not be generated dynamically inside CUDA kernels.

- Updated static-hybrid history budget calculation. Required history entries are now estimated per rank as `states_before_target_beam + local_beam_width * max(depth_limit - target_beam_depth - BEAM_SOLVED_NEIGHBORHOOD_RADIUS - BEAM_STREAM2_SUFFIX_RADIUS, 0)`, with a conservative `MOVE_COUNT` upper-bound estimate for the pre-target frontier because actual frontier sizes are unavailable before static allocation. - Launched Kaggle version 122 for puzzle IDs `991..999` with `BEAM_WIDTH=2**26+15_506_660`, `DEPTH_LIMIT=72`, no per-puzzle timeout, static-hybrid history budget `28 GiB RAM + 59 GiB disk`, K1 radius `4`, K2 disabled, and selected T4x2 concurrency config `SHARD_COUNT=64`, `STREAM4_ACTIVE_SORT_SLOTS=4`, `B_MICRO=2048`, `STREAM1_CONCURRENCY=4`, `STREAM3_RING_SLOTS=4`, `STREAM4_BATCH_CANDIDATES=196608`, `STREAM4_TRIGGER_CANDIDATES=393216`. Kaggle marked the notebook as failed with `Your notebook tried to use more disk space than is available.` The cause is the configured total disk arena `59*1024**3 = 63.35GB`; the code divides this across two ranks, creating two `31.68GB` `/tmp/beam_history_arena/rank_*` files, so total static history arena alone exceeds Kaggle's displayed disk quota before counting repo/build/log/output files. Report saved as `test_results/kaggle_v122_disk_quota_failure_2026-05-28.md`.
- Fixed Kaggle notebooks only after v122 disk quota failure. Added a post-config/pre-compilation preflight cell that cleans stale `/tmp/beam_history_*` and configured `HISTORY_DISK_PATH`, computes Stream1/Stream3/Stream4 config guards, effective beam/local beam, shard capacity, static history RAM/disk arenas, estimated required history bytes, current free disk under `/tmp`, and raises before clone/build if the configured run cannot fit. - Implemented a manifest-driven Stream1 MLP runtime interface. Verification passed: old model export reproduced current `.fp16` files byte-for-byte; new model exported as `hd1=2048`, `hd2=1024`, `nrd=3`; Docker CUDA build targets `production_runner`, `stream_benchmark`, and `stream1_cuda_tests` passed.

- Kaggle version 127 timing diagnostic failed before depth 0 because the debug-timing build exceeded the T4 manual GPU budget by `12,557,488` bytes (`required=15189449904`, `budget=15176892416`, `free_before=15445327872`, `headroom=268435456`). - Kaggle version 128 timing diagnostic passed the manual budget check but failed before depth 0 with `cudaGraphInstantiate: out of memory`. To free graph-instantiation headroom while preserving Stream1 concurrency, Stream3 ring slots, Stream4 batch/trigger sizes, and active sort slots, adjusted only `SHARD_CAPACITY_SCALE_PPM` from `1125000` to `1100000` for the next timing diagnostic rerun.
- Kaggle version 129 still failed before depth 0 with `cudaGraphInstantiate: out of memory` at `SHARD_CAPACITY_SCALE_PPM=1100000`. Both Kaggle notebooks now reduce only the diagnostic beam by `1,000,000`, from `BEAM_WIDTH=2**26+15_506_660` to `BEAM_WIDTH=2**26+14_506_660` (`81,615,524`), keeping `DEBUG_STREAM_TIMING=True` and `DEPTH_LIMIT=9`.
- Kaggle version 131 validated the user-requested beam-only reduction on T4x2. Interpretation: Stream4 is costly, but the critical path is currently Stream1/2 plus Stream3 rather than Stream4 alone. Report saved as `test_results/kaggle_v131_timing_debug_analysis_2026-05-30.md`.
- Prepared a benchmark-only Kaggle measurement pass for the new Stream1 model. `stream_benchmark` now sweeps `B_MICRO=[2048,4096,8192,16384]` and `STREAM1_CONCURRENCY=[1,2,4,8]`; the isolated Stream3 benchmark uses `ring_slot_count=4`, so `B_MICRO=2048` matches the current production `STREAM3_BATCH_CANDIDATES=196608`. Docker verification: `stream_benchmark` target rebuilt successfully.

Verification passed: Docker rebuilt `stream_benchmark` and a local full `stream_benchmark` smoke completed through Stream1/2/3/4.

- Kaggle version 134 failed with `KernelWorkerStatus.ERROR` and the explicit Kaggle failure message `Your notebook tried to use more disk space than is available.` No CUDA fatal log was available from the CLI; `kaggle kernels files` listed only small output files. Diagnosis: current `HISTORY_DISK_BYTES=59*1024**3` config budgets about `59 GiB` total static-hybrid disk history across two ranks, which exceeds Kaggle's effective notebook disk quota once repo/build/logs/output are included, especially during a long `DEPTH_LIMIT=72` run. Report saved as `test_results/kaggle_v134_disk_quota_failure_2026-05-30.md`.
- Implemented Stream1-only support for model manifests with `output_dim=1`. Runtime now auto-detects `output_dim=1`, treats `BEAM_B_MICRO` as the Stream1 row budget, runs `floor(BEAM_B_MICRO / 24)` parent states per Stream1 job, expands parent+move features inside the Stream1 input kernel without writing child `State128` arrays, preserves the existing candidate score order for Stream2/3/4/5, and keeps `output_dim=24` behavior unchanged. The heavy hidden/residual MLP stages still use CUTLASS; the final `N=1` score head uses a small direct CUDA kernel after the existing CUTLASS profile produced a misaligned-address failure for `N=1`. Verification passed: Docker build targets `stream1_cuda_tests`, `production_runner`, and `stream_benchmark`; Docker `stream1_cuda_tests`; old `output_dim=24` production smoke `production_runner 0 1 4096`; `git diff --check`. Report saved as `test_results/stream1_output_dim1_support_2026-05-30.md`.
- Added `STREAM1_MODEL_WEIGHTS_URL` as the first Kaggle notebook config line in both notebook copies. Empty value keeps repository `stream1_weights`; non-empty value downloads a `.pth` URL or copies an absolute Kaggle/local path, runs `tools/export_stream1_mlp.py`, and replaces `REPO_DIR/stream1_weights` before build/run. The exporter infers `output_dim` from weights, so the same Kaggle notebook supports Stream1 models with one output or twenty-four outputs. Verification passed: both notebooks parse as JSON and all code cells compile. Report saved as `test_results/kaggle_model_weights_url_config_2026-05-30.md`.
- Added `hpc/solve_then_reflect.sh`, a MEPhI 8xA100 SLURM workflow that solves

- Removed the invalid Stream3/Stream4 guard requiring `STREAM4_BATCH_CANDIDATES >= STREAM3_BATCH_CANDIDATES`. The runtime and Kaggle preflight now enforce the actual physical-buffer invariant `STREAM3_BATCH_CANDIDATES <= SHARD_CAPACITY_CANDIDATES`, while keeping Stream4 batch/trigger capacity guards. Kaggle preflight also now infers local `.pth` `output_dim` when possible, converts `B_MICRO` row budget to parent batch for one-output models, and accepts a directory path by resolving the first nested `.pth`. Verification passed: notebook compile, guard scan, `git diff --check`, and Docker `production_runner 0 1 4096` with `STREAM3_BATCH_CANDIDATES=1536`, `STREAM4_BATCH_CANDIDATES=1024`, `SHARD_CAPACITY_CANDIDATES=2048`. - Profiled local Docker run for `weights_megaminx2048_512_8_e4000.pth` (`hd1=2048`, `hd2=512`, `nrd=8`, `output_dim=1`).
