Production `beam=2**22`, `depth_limit=7`, `B_MICRO=49152`, `STREAM1_CONCURRENCY=4`, `STREAM3_RING_SLOTS=4`, `SHARD_COUNT=1` showed depth 6 wall `162.755s`, `stream12_ms=155846`, and `stream4_ms=31789.6`, so the slowdown is primarily Stream1/2 graph time dominated by one-output inference; the tested user config also had `WORLD_SIZE=1`, so only one GPU/rank is used. Report saved as `test_results/local_output_dim1_profile_2026-05-30.md`.
- Ran Kaggle-only `WORLD_SIZE=1`, `BEAM_WIDTH=2**22`, `DEPTH_LIMIT=7` speed diagnostics after the user forbade further Docker testing. Version 141 showed the main `output_dim=24` slowdown came from `SHARD_COUNT=1` / `STREAM4_ACTIVE_SORT_SLOTS=1`: depth6 improved from `23.147s` at `sh1/slot1` to `11.600s` at `sh8/slot4`. - Added native Molab `sm_120` build support. `BEAM_CUDA_ARCHITECTURES` is now a CMake cache string so Molab can pass `-DBEAM_CUDA_ARCHITECTURES=120`; added `cuda/cub_compat.hpp` to keep Stream3/static-memory CUB reduce paths building across older CUB and CUDA 13.3 CCCL, where `cub/iterator/constant_input_iterator.cuh` and `cub::Sum` are no longer available. Molab CUDA 13.3 build target `production_runner` succeeded for `compute_120/sm_120` on RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell, and a small manual smoke `production_runner 0 1 4096` returned `smoke_rc=0`. Transformer Engine was not installed in the Molab environment, so no Stream1 TE path was added. Report saved as `test_results/molab_sm120_native_build_2026-06-03.md`.
- Ran Molab native `sm_120` depth-log beam search for puzzle `992` with the current `output_dim=24` model and `beam=100000000`. The run reached full beam at depth 6 (`next_frontier_size=100007936`, `depth_sec=6.36314`) but failed at `depth_start=7` with `cudaStreamSynchronize final materialize: an illegal memory access was encountered`; `MAIN_RC=-6`, elapsed `33.752s`, no solution. Report saved as `test_results/molab_p992_beam100m_depthlogs_2026-06-03.md`.
- Diagnosed the Molab `p992`/`beam=100000000` crash with a native `sm_120` debug build using `BEAM_DEBUG_FINAL_VALIDATE=ON`. The validator reproduced the depth-7 failure before materialization with `invalid_count=278`; the first bad final request had `parent_idx=6438027017329790029` while `current_frontier_size=100007936`, with valid `target_local_idx`, move, and request slot. This localizes the illegal memory access to a corrupt upstream `CandidateMeta.parent_idx` in the selected survivor/final stream, not to final materialization indexing itself. Report saved as `test_results/molab_p992_final_validate_2026-06-03.md`.
- Added a debug-only pre-final survivor scan under `BEAM_DEBUG_FINAL_VALIDATE` and reran the Molab `p992`/`beam=100000000` diagnostic. The scan fails before `final_filter_load_balance_cuda`, proving the corrupt `CandidateMeta` is already in live `survivor_shard`: `invalid_selected_count=104`, `first_shard=3`, `first_local=233861`, `parent_idx=6293324596111102288`, `current_frontier_size=21612910`, `score_key=0`, `route_packed=0`. The bad bytes look like stale `State128`/next-frontier materialization data being counted as live survivor candidates after phase3 scratch overlay, so the next root-cause target is Stream3/Stream4 count/write agreement for survivor slots. Verification: Docker debug `production_runner` build passed. Report updated at `test_results/molab_p992_final_validate_2026-06-03.md`.
- Fixed the root cause of the Molab `p992`/`beam=100000000` survivor corruption. Persistent stream storage now starts after the full `layout_final_budget_bytes = max(layout_phase2_select_bytes, layout_phase3_materialize_bytes)` prefix instead of after only phase 2, preventing phase-3 `next_frontier_states_tmp` `State128` writes from overlaying `survivor_shard`, clean counts, and shard histogram state between depths. Updated `static_memory_cuda_tests` to enforce that the full final layout does not overlap persistent stream storage and documented the invariant in `ARCHITECTURE_NEED.md`. Verification passed: `git diff --check`, Docker debug build target `static_memory_cuda_tests production_runner`, `static_memory_cuda_tests`, a small Docker `production_runner 0 1 4096` smoke, and Molab native `sm_120` `production_runner 992 8 100000000` with `BEAM_DEBUG_FINAL_VALIDATE=ON`, which completed depth 7 and returned `CMD_RC 0` instead of the prior pre-final survivor validation failure / final materialization illegal access. Report saved as `test_results/layout_final_budget_survivor_fix_2026-06-03.md`.
- Extended `tools/stream_benchmark.cu` Stream1 sweep to include `B_MICRO=32768,65536` and `STREAM1_CONCURRENCY=16,32`. - Extended the MEPhI 8xA100 launch script to use the same user-local `ninja-venv` for PyTorch/NCCL runtime dependencies. The script now validates `${NINJA_VENV_DIR}/bin/python`, `nvidia/nccl/include/nccl.h`, and `nvidia/nccl/lib/libnccl.so.2`, passes explicit `NCCL_INCLUDE_DIR` and `NCCL_LIBRARY` to CMake, prepends the NCCL lib directory to `LD_LIBRARY_PATH`, and runs torchrun through `${NINJA_VENV_DIR}/bin/python`.
- Aligned the MEPhI 8xA100 launcher with the published Kaggle `cayley-beam-gpu-runner` runtime derivation: effective world size is now `TORCHRUN_NNODES * TORCHRUN_NPROC_PER_NODE`, global beam is rounded up to `world_size * shard_count * STREAM4_BATCH_ALIGNMENT`, local beam/logical shard size are derived from that value, and `BEAM_SHARD_CAPACITY_CANDIDATES` is computed with `SHARD_CAPACITY_SCALE_PPM` and alignment. The script now preflights `STREAM4_BATCH_CANDIDATES <= shard_capacity` and `STREAM4_BATCH_CANDIDATES <= STREAM4_TRIGGER_CANDIDATES <= shard_capacity` before submitting the binary through torchrun.
- Added the missing Kaggle-style C++ NCCL rendezvous file wiring to the MEPhI torchrun launcher: before torchrun, the script removes `${JOB_DIR}/beam_solver_nccl_${SLURM_JOB_ID:-manual}.bin`, exports it as `BEAM_NCCL_ID_FILE`, and prints the path. This lets `production_runner` use the same shared NCCL unique-id file contract under torchrun that the Kaggle notebook used with manually spawned rank processes.
- Changed the MEPhI torchrun launcher logging to match the Kaggle notebook's readable-rank behavior. Torchrun now launches a small shell wrapper: rank 0 writes to the main `tee`/SLURM log, while ranks 1..N write themselves to `${LOG_DIR}/ranks_${SLURM_JOB_ID:-manual}/rank${RANK}.log`, avoiding interleaved multi-rank depth lines in the main output.
- Added a MEPhI 8xA100 tuning launcher set. `tune_8xa100_stream1.sh` builds/runs `stream_benchmark` and writes `logs/best_stream1.env`; `tune_8xa100_pipeline.sh` runs 8-rank torchrun production sweeps to depth 12 across shard count, Stream3 ring slots, Stream4 batch, and trigger size, records pass/fail plus average full-depth runtime in TSV, and writes `logs/best_pipeline.env`; `start_8xa100_best.sh` sources that best config for the final production run. Shared MEPhI preflight/build/torchrun/capacity logic now lives in `hpc/mephi_8xa100_common.sh`.
- Fixed MEPhI tuning launcher issues found on `basis`: SLURM spools submitted scripts under `/var/lib/slurm/slurmd/job...`, so the tuning scripts now source `mephi_8xa100_common.sh` from `${SLURM_SUBMIT_DIR:-$(pwd)}` instead of `BASH_SOURCE[0]`; `tune_8xa100_stream1.sh` now parses `b_micro=` from `stream_benchmark` output when writing `logs/best_stream1.env`.
- Fixed the MEPhI pipeline sweep average-depth parser (`printf "%.6f", sum / n`) and added automatic per-config GPU utilization logging. `beam_torchrun_production` now runs a background `nvidia-smi` sampler every 5 seconds while torchrun is active and writes `logs/tuning_<job>/nvidia_smi_<run_tag>.log`, then stops the sampler after the config exits.
- Hardened MEPhI pipeline history cleanup: the sweep now clears history through `beam_safe_clear_history_contents`, which requires `realpath(HISTORY_DIR) == realpath(JOB_DIR)/history` and removes only first-level entries inside that directory. This replaces the direct `rm -rf "${HISTORY_DIR:?}/"*` sweep cleanup.
- Added `tools/compare_solution_lengths.py`, a lightweight solution-length comparison CLI. It compares move counts only, without puzzle validation, from a baseline submission CSV against either another submission CSV or `solve_then_reflect` SLURM logs; output includes old length, new length, delta, source, solution, and an aggregate delta/improved/equal/worsened summary.
- Extended Stream1 model support for Artgor-style `ResMLPDistance` exports. `tools/export_stream1_mlp.py` now supports `--format resmlp-layernorm` and `--dtype fp16|bf16`, folds the embedding plus first linear layer into the existing position-class input table, exports LayerNorm parameters, and writes manifests with `normalization=layernorm`. Stream1 runtime manifests now accept `dtype=bf16` and optional LayerNorm metadata, and the main CUTLASS Stream1 path inserts LayerNorm kernels between GEMM stages when required while preserving the existing no-LayerNorm fp16 behavior. The LayerNorm input path keeps folded input pre-bias and applies `input_bias` only inside LayerNorm to match PyTorch `Linear -> LayerNorm -> ReLU` semantics; the existing fp16 no-LayerNorm folded-input path keeps `half2` bias/weight reads.
- Downloaded Kaggle checkpoint `artgor/megaminx-tpu-artifacts:m_az_v4_v_only.pt`, exported it as repository weights under `stream1_weights_artgor_bf16/` using `--format resmlp-layernorm --dtype bf16`, and prepared it for MEPhI 8xA100 puzzle `991` runs. `hpc/mephi_8xa100_common.sh` now respects a caller-provided `BEAM_WEIGHT_DIR`, so cluster jobs can select the Artgor weights without replacing the default `stream1_weights` directory.
- Fixed MEPhI shell-side Stream1 row budgeting for `output_dim=1` models. The C++ runtime already converts `BEAM_B_MICRO` from a row budget to an effective parent batch via `stream1_parent_batch_from_row_budget`; the MEPhI shell derivation now mirrors that by reading the selected weight manifest, deriving `BEAM_PARENT_BATCH_EFFECTIVE=BEAM_B_MICRO/24` for one-output models, and using that value for `STREAM3_BATCH_CANDIDATES`/preflight logs. This prevents Artgor one-output runs from allocating Stream1 buffers as if `BEAM_B_MICRO` were still a parent count.
- Added `hpc/solve_reflected_only.sh`, a 24-hour MEPhI SLURM script that takes `ORIGINAL_SOLUTION`, writes only the reflected synthetic puzzle, solves that reflected puzzle, and prints the inverted reflected candidate for the original puzzle. This avoids re-solving the original when a valid original solution is already available from rank logs.
- Added an IHES cube MEPhI launcher under `hpc/ihes_cube_model/`. It expects a real `.pth` model at `/mnt/pool/6/vokirova/beam8a100/ihes_cube_model/model.pth`, exports it to `stream1_weights_ihes_bf16` with `--format batchnorm-folded --dtype bf16 --num-classes 72`, compiles `production_runner` with `BEAM_PUZZLE_INFO_JSON` pointing at IHES `puzzle_info.json`, and runs through the existing 8xA100 torchrun path. `hpc/mephi_8xa100_common.sh` now passes optional `BEAM_PUZZLE_INFO_JSON` into CMake and derives one-output Stream1 row budgeting from the active puzzle move count rather than hardcoding 24 moves, so IHES 18-move puzzles get correct `BEAM_PARENT_BATCH_EFFECTIVE` and `STREAM3_BATCH_CANDIDATES`.
- Published the IHES model checkpoint as GitHub Release asset `ihes-p888-model/p888-t000_1778521793_e32692.pth` and updated `hpc/ihes_cube_model/ihes_cube_model.sh` to download it automatically into `model.pth` when absent. The script prefers `gh release download` and falls back to a Python GitHub API download so cluster runs remain GitHub-driven without committing the 61 MB binary into git history.
- Restored the shared MEPhI common script's default `CUTLASS_DIR` to `/mnt/pool/3/vokirova/cutlass`, matching the existing MEPhI setup docs and older launcher. The previous `${REPO_DIR}/external/cutlass` default was wrong for this repository because `external/` is ignored and CUTLASS is not a git submodule.
- Split IHES cluster setup from compute launch. `hpc/ihes_cube_model/prepare_ihes_cube_model.sh` now performs the GitHub release model download, Kaggle IHES data download, and bf16 Stream1 weight export from the visible `basis` shell before `sbatch`. `hpc/ihes_cube_model/ihes_cube_model.sh` now mirrors the megaminx launch rule: it performs no network downloads inside the SLURM compute job, only validates prepared `model.pth`, IHES data, and exported weights before compiling/running. Docker `bash -n` passed for the prepare/start/common scripts, and grep confirmed network download commands are absent from the SLURM script.
- Uploaded `cayleypy-ihes-cube.zip` to the same GitHub Release as the IHES model and removed Kaggle CLI usage from `hpc/ihes_cube_model/prepare_ihes_cube_model.sh`. IHES data preparation is now GitHub-only: the prepare script downloads `cayleypy-ihes-cube.zip` from release `ihes-p888-model`, unzips it locally under the job directory, and then exports Stream1 weights. Verification confirmed the release has both assets and `prepare.sh` contains no Kaggle download command.
- Added TensorOp alignment padding to the BatchNorm-folded Stream1 exporter. Hidden dimensions are rounded up to multiples of 8 in exported weights/manifests while original dimensions are recorded as `original_hd1/original_hd2`; padded channels use zero weights and zero biases so model outputs are unchanged. The IHES p888 model now exports as `hd1=2560` and `hd2=224` from original `2556/218`. `prepare_ihes_cube_model.sh` now re-exports existing weights when a stale manifest has non-8-aligned hidden sizes.
- Fixed the IHES path-trace prefinal debug scanner after the distributed tracker reached depth 1 but crashed while logging an invalid best candidate. The debug-only survivor scan now carries `best_score`, `best_index`, and `best_candidate` together during block reduction, and `production_runner` guards debug `move_name` logging against invalid unpacked move ids. This changes only diagnostics; the search pipeline, Stream3/Stream4 data flow, and finalization architecture are unchanged.
- Added a debug-only IHES tracked-path host check. When `BEAM_DEBUG_PATH_TRACE` and `BEAM_TRACK_SOLUTION_PATH` are enabled, `production_runner` now prints whether the tracked final host state equals `central_state`, whether tracked/central padding bytes are zero, whether final and central hashes match, and whether the final hash exists in the host solved-neighborhood map. This is diagnostic only and does not change search, scoring, buffering, or solved detection.
- Fixed IHES data path selection in cluster runs. `production_runner` now reads `puzzle_info.json` from `BEAM_PUZZLE_INFO_JSON` and `test.csv` from `BEAM_TEST_CSV` when provided, falling back to repository `data/` for existing Megaminx runs. The IHES launcher exports `BEAM_TEST_CSV="${DATA_DIR}/test.csv"` so generators, central state, and initial puzzle rows all come from the same IHES data directory.
- Added `hpc/ihes_cube_model/ihes_solve_then_reflect.sh`, an IHES-specific MEPhI SLURM wrapper that uses the prepared IHES model/weight directories, copies IHES data into a per-job `data-${SLURM_JOB_ID}` directory, solves the original puzzle, writes a reflected synthetic puzzle into that per-job `test.csv`, solves the reflected puzzle, and prints the inverted reflected candidate for the original. The wrapper keeps the existing CUDA solver architecture unchanged and only adds shell/Python orchestration around the two standard `production_runner` launches.
- Replaced the root `README.md` scaffold text with a user-facing run guide that
  explains generic 1-GPU execution, multi-GPU `torchrun`, SLURM cluster launch
  patterns, IHES data/model setup, Kaggle notebook usage, logs, cleanup, and
  development tests without relying on site-specific cluster rules.
- Added host-side segment repair support. `production_runner` now accepts
  `BEAM_START_STATE_TEXT`/`BEAM_START_STATE_FILE` and
  `BEAM_TARGET_STATE_TEXT`/`BEAM_TARGET_STATE_FILE`; K1 solved-neighborhood BFS
  builds from the target override, while default behavior still targets the
  central state. Added `tools/generate_segment_repair_jobs.py` to turn existing
  solution paths into synthetic start/target repair jobs, and
  `hpc/ihes_cube_model/ihes_segment_repair.sh` to run one IHES repair row and
  print the spliced candidate.
- Updated the segment repair generator so the default repair window can be
  derived from beam width: `search_depth = max d where move_count**d <=
  beam_width`, and `window = K1 + search_depth`. This matches the intended
  BFS-like depth where the beam can hold the complete frontier layer.
- Extended IHES segment repair into a per-solution workflow. The generator now
  writes `solution_jobs.csv` plus segment rows keyed by `solution_job_id`, with
  block stepping by `K1 + search_depth` by default and optional diversity
  windows via `--search-depths 1-7`. Added
  `hpc/ihes_cube_model/ihes_solution_repair.sh`, where one SLURM array task
  repairs one original solution: it compiles once, runs all segments for that
  solution sequentially, records segment metadata, stitches candidates per
  repair depth, and prints the best candidate.
- Added the IHES unique-solutions input file
  `hpc/ihes_cube_model/solv_uniq.csv` so cluster repair jobs can be prepared
  through GitHub without manually uploading the Telegram copy.
- Added `SOLUTION_ROW_OFFSET` support to
  `hpc/ihes_cube_model/ihes_solution_repair.sh` so MEPhI SLURM chunks can use
  array indices `0-999` repeatedly while selecting later rows from
  `solution_repair_solutions.csv`.
- Added prebuilt IHES repair runner support. `prepare_ihes_prebuilt_runner.sh`
  builds one stable `production_runner` under `prebuilt-a100-ihes`, and
  `ihes_solution_repair.sh` can reuse it through `BEAM_PREBUILT_RUNNER` instead
  of running CMake/NVCC inside every solution-repair array task.
- Added an explicit IHES solution repair execution mode switch:
  `BEAM_SOLUTION_REPAIR_MODE=plan` runs all segments through one outer
  `torchrun`, while `BEAM_SOLUTION_REPAIR_MODE=legacy` preserves the previous
  one-`torchrun`-per-segment behavior. Unknown modes fail immediately; there is
  no automatic fallback.
- Added `run_solution_repair_plan.sh` and common `beam_torchrun_segment_plan`
  orchestration so plan mode runs the whole segment list inside one outer
  distributed launch. Both plan and legacy modes now set
  `BEAM_HISTORY_DISK_PATH` per segment so history directories remain isolated;
  plan mode leaves cleanup to the existing job-level safe cleanup after the
  outer distributed run exits.
- Fixed plan-mode launcher execution by invoking `run_solution_repair_plan.sh`
  through `/bin/bash` inside the distributed wrapper, so it does not depend on
  the repository file executable bit after `git fetch/reset`.
- Added `hpc/ihes_cube_model/prepare_solution_repair_single_window.sh` for the
  current IHES repair workflow. It prepares `solution_repair_*` CSV files with
  exactly one block window, `window = K1 + 7` by default, instead of generating
  the previous diversity sweep over `K1+1..K1+7`.
- Replaced the plan-mode repair launcher internals with a native lightweight
  rank launcher while leaving legacy mode on `torchrun`. Plan mode now starts
  `WORLD_SIZE_EFFECTIVE` shell ranks directly with `RANK`, `LOCAL_RANK`, and
  `WORLD_SIZE` set, writes only rank 0 to the main log, and stores nonzero ranks
  under the existing rank log directory.
- Added an explicit `BEAM_SOLUTION_REPAIR_MODE=legacy_native` mode for the
  current IHES solution repair workflow. It keeps the per-segment legacy
  execution model, but launches each segment through a lightweight native
  8-rank shell launcher instead of `torchrun`; existing `legacy` and `plan`
  modes are unchanged.
- Added a dedicated resident IHES solution-repair mode. `production_runner`
  now supports `BEAM_REPAIR_SOLUTIONS_CSV`, builds block segment tasks directly
  from `solv_uniq.csv` plus `BEAM_TEST_CSV`, and runs them in one process/rank
  group while reusing CUDA/NCCL/model/static allocations. The solved-neighborhood
  device table now has a stable arena path: later segment targets rebuild the
  CPU K1 table and overwrite the same device pointers, so CUDA graph templates
  can be reused as long as the radius/bucket/slot shape stays fixed. Added
  `BEAM_SOLUTION_REPAIR_MODE=resident` to `hpc/ihes_cube_model/ihes_solution_repair.sh`
  to launch this path and write a per-segment TSV.
- Fixed resident repair input parsing for the actual `solv_uniq.csv` format,
  which is a JSON object mapping puzzle ids to arrays of solution paths, not
  only a two-column CSV.
- Fixed resident repair stable solved-neighborhood reuse across target states.
  Subsequent K1 tables are now packed into the first segment's fixed bucket
  count so CUDA graph templates can keep the same device pointers and bucket
  mask even when a later target has a slightly different deduplicated entry
  count.
- Added resident repair CPU K1 prefetching. In resident mode a bounded host RAM
  queue now prepares solved-neighborhood K1 tables for upcoming segment tasks
  while the GPU is solving the current one. `BEAM_REPAIR_K1_PREFETCH_TASKS`
  controls the number of ready tasks kept in RAM, defaulting to 10; `0`
  disables it. The stable device arena remains a single table, with a small
  configurable bucket-count reserve via `BEAM_REPAIR_K1_STABLE_BUCKET_SCALE_PPM`.

- Fixed IHES solve-bucket reflected aggregation safety. The wrapper now normalizes each known path orientation against the original puzzle before reflected-state synthesis, and validates every found path against the original puzzle before writing aggregate improvements, preventing false reflected improvements from mixed-orientation solv_uniq.csv entries.

- Added hpc/ihes_cube_model/ihes_solve_bucket_from_scratch.sh, an IHES bucket workflow that uses solv_uniq.csv only to select puzzle ids with baseline lengths 23,24, solves original states from scratch, stores all bucket solutions, and only runs reflected buckets from actually found original solutions until a shorter-than-baseline solution is found.

- Improved hpc/ihes_cube_model/ihes_solve_bucket_from_scratch.sh resume behavior and reflected-source deduplication. Reflected sources are now deduplicated by the reflected state hash, not just by identical path text; stable FRESH_RUN_TAG output/progress files prevent result truncation on restart; original and reflected done markers let a restarted job continue the current puzzle instead of re-running completed sources.

- Added optional public results publishing to hpc/ihes_cube_model/ihes_solve_bucket_from_scratch.sh. When PUBLISH_RESULTS_REPO_URL or an existing PUBLISH_RESULTS_DIR checkout is configured, each completed puzzle writes per-puzzle solutions.tsv, unique_solutions.tsv, metadata.env, summary.md, plus global IHES index/improvements TSV files and commits/pushes them to the separate results repository. The solver/CUDA architecture is unchanged; publishing is best-effort so completed local logs are retained if GitHub auth or network fails. Verification note: test_results/ihes_bucket_public_results_publish_2026-06-26.md.

- Updated IHES fresh bucket array documentation to use one shared precompiled production_runner. The documented flow submits prepare_ihes_prebuilt_runner.sh first, then submits the puzzle array with --dependency=afterok:<prebuild_job> and BEAM_PREBUILT_RUNNER=/mnt/pool/6/vokirova/beam8a100/ihes_cube_model/prebuilt-a100-ihes/production_runner so each one-puzzle task reuses the same binary instead of rebuilding.

- Fixed IHES live results publishing index generation after raw/backfill metadata added extra keys. The fresh bucket publisher now includes variants/source_files columns in data/ihes_cube/index.tsv and filters metadata rows to declared fields before writing index/improvements, so older per-puzzle metadata cannot crash publishing.

- Fixed the registered `stream1_transformer_cuda_tests` clean-checkout behavior. The CUDA test now skips with `stream1_transformer_cuda_tests=skip missing_reference_fixture` and exits 0 when the ignored p900 reference fixture manifest or reference JSON is absent, while preserving the full fixture-backed p900 reference verification when those files exist. Verification note: `test_results/stream1_transformer_forward_2026-06-29.md`.

- Fixed Stream1 piece-transformer runtime weight sizing for `fast_slot_projected`. Runtime budget estimates now use the loader/exporter contract shape `max_piece_size * num_classes * d_model`, and the dispatcher CUDA fixture allocates the same shape. Verification note: `test_results/stream1_transformer_weight_estimate_contract_2026-06-29.md`.

- Made `tests/dispatcher_cuda_tests.cu` runtime estimate environment setup portable. The test now uses a scoped helper that sets the same BEAM_* overrides with `_putenv_s` on Windows and `setenv`/`unsetenv` elsewhere, restoring previous values or clearing test-only values after the estimate scope.
- 2026-06-29: Stabilized dispatcher CUDA tests after portable env setup by explicitly setting Stream4 trigger candidates and removing a brittle Stream4 launch-count upper bound.
- Optimized the Stream1 piece-transformer attention path by replacing global `attention_scores_probs` scratch with a shared-memory `(row, head)` attention tile and removing redundant transformer scratch clears. Local Docker CUDA verification passed `stream1_transformer_cuda_tests`, `contract_tests`, and `dispatcher_cuda_tests`; local benchmark evidence is in `test_results/stream1_transformer_shared_attention_2026-06-30.md`. This improves the first transformer benchmark point but does not yet reach the expected MLP-relative speed envelope.
- Replaced the Stream1 piece-transformer attention kernel with a warp-specialized short-sequence kernel: one block owns `(row, head)`, each warp owns one query, `head_dim=32` dot products use shuffle reductions, and softmax scores stay in per-warp shared memory. Local benchmark best improved to `304663.2` candidates/s at `B_MICRO=1024, concurrency=1`; `stream1_transformer_cuda_tests`, `contract_tests`, and `dispatcher_cuda_tests` passed. Evidence is in `test_results/stream1_transformer_warp_attention_2026-06-30.md`.
- Ran Kaggle 2xT4 benchmark version 3 against commit `a290524`. The warp-specialized Stream1 transformer attention backend improved best aggregate throughput from `232720.5` to `468478.3` candidates/s (`2.01x`). Best T4 point is now `B_MICRO=1024`, `STREAM1_CONCURRENCY=1`. Report: `test_results/stream1_transformer_kaggle_benchmark_v3_2026-06-30.md`.
- Corrected the Stream1 piece-transformer speed interpretation after PyTorch fast-path evidence: the ~5x MLP-relative target remains the practical envelope, and the current native gap should be profiled as implementation overhead rather than explained away as intrinsic model cost. Updated transformer benchmark reports accordingly; no CUDA/runtime behavior changed.
- Rewrote the Stream1 piece-transformer attention hot path to use CUTLASS tensor-core QK/PV attention instead of the scalar/warp attention kernel. The current execution path uses device pointer-array scratch plus `GemmArray` QK/PV launches, padded half score/prob/V scratch, and chunked attention batches so `B_MICRO=8192` no longer fails at `8192*8=65536` attention matrices. Local Docker verification passed `stream1_transformer_cuda_tests`; best local T4 sweep point is `B_MICRO=512, concurrency=1` at `583142.3` candidates/s. Nsight now shows the remaining bottleneck is mostly linear GEMMs and unfused elementwise transformer kernels rather than the old attention kernel. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_tensor_attention_rewrite_2026-06-30.md`.
- Rewrote the Stream1 piece-transformer attention path into a clean tensor-core implementation without pointer-array scratch or CUTLASS `GemmArray`. The new path keeps QKV/FF/output GEMMs unchanged, applies QKV bias in-place, uses per-head CUTLASS `GemmBatched` QK/PV with one padded score/prob/V scratch, and preserves the MLP Stream1 path. Local Docker verification passed `stream1_transformer_cuda_tests`; local fp16 benchmark points reached `577909.4` candidates/s at `B_MICRO=512, concurrency=1`, `592946.4` at `1024x1`, and `602873.7` at `512x2`. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_clean_tensor_attention_2026-07-01.md`.
- Ran Kaggle 2xT4 benchmark version 5 against commit `78052c5` with an explicit notebook commit check. Kaggle completed successfully, but clean tensor attention only reached `821205.4` aggregate candidates/s (`406086.6` on GPU0, `415118.8` on GPU1), about `1.01x` v4 and still only `0.64-0.66x` of the PyTorch `batch_process` fast path per T4. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_kaggle_benchmark_v5_2026-07-01.md`.

- Fixed the Torch-side Stream1 MLP benchmark parser to accept `initial_state_id` from `data/test.csv`, matching the native benchmark input schema. Kaggle 2xT4 v1 had completed native CUTLASS MLP measurements but failed before Torch timing because the parser only accepted `id/puzzle_id/ID`. Evidence: `test_results/kaggle_t4_mlp_native_vs_torch_v1_failure_2026-07-01.md`.
- Ran Kaggle 2xT4 MLP native-vs-PyTorch benchmark version 2 against source tag `stream1-mlp-torch-kaggle-v2-code` (`cc90a3d`). Kaggle completed successfully. Best native CUTLASS MLP reached `16.42M` candidates/s on GPU0 and `15.73M` on GPU1; best PyTorch preindexed MLP reached `8.13M` and `6.76M` candidates/s. Native is `2.02x`/`2.33x` faster per GPU and `2.16x` faster in aggregate. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_mlp_native_vs_torch_kaggle_v2_2026-07-01.md`.
- Added kaggle_transformer_inference_speed/transformer_inference_profile.py and test_results/stream1_transformer_pytorch_gap_analysis_2026-07-02.md to compare the PyTorch fast SDPA path against the native Stream1 piece-transformer path. The analysis identifies native attention decomposition, per-head QK/PV launches, separate softmax/V-pack, and unfused epilogues as the current T4 performance gap; no solver runtime or MLP path behavior changed.
- Added an explicit Stream1 piece-transformer attention backend split: fp16 uses the existing T4/SM75-safe CUTLASS tensor QK/softmax/PV path, while bf16 on SM80+ routes to a separate `cuda/stream1_transformer_fmha.cu` CUTLASS example-41 FMHA path with packed BMHK Q/K/V. The piece-transformer executor now lives in `cuda/stream1_transformer.cu`, leaving the MLP Stream1 implementation in `cuda/stream1.cu`; this gives the fused-transformer work a separate backend boundary for future epilogue/layout/graph optimization. The FMHA code is fail-closed when CUTLASS FMHA headers or SM80+ are unavailable, and the MLP path is not touched. Verification passed `stream1_transformer_cuda_tests`, `contract_tests`, and `dispatcher_cuda_tests`; bf16 FMHA smoke also passed after the split. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_cutlass_fmha_backend_split_2026-07-02.md`. Local performance numbers were gathered on a throttled RTX 3070 Laptop GPU and should not be treated as an A100/T4 performance gate.

- Added a transformer-local residual GEMM epilogue path in `cuda/stream1_transformer.cu` for attention-output and FF2 projections: CUTLASS now computes `D = A * B + residual`, followed by a narrow bias-add kernel. This removes the previous large intermediate branch materialization for those residual projections, leaves the MLP path untouched, and keeps fp16/T4 and bf16/FMHA backend selection fail-closed. Verification passed `stream1_transformer_cuda_tests`, `contract_tests`, and `dispatcher_cuda_tests`; local smoke reports reached `575995.4` candidates/s for fp16 staged `512x1` and `675808.8` candidates/s for bf16 FMHA `512x1`. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_cutlass_fmha_backend_split_2026-07-02.md`.

- Fused the Stream1 piece-transformer FF1 projection epilogue in `cuda/stream1_transformer.cu`: CUTLASS now computes `GEMM + bias + SiLU` through `GemmUniversalWithBroadcast` and `LinearCombinationBiasElementwise`, while the MLP path and residual GEMM path remain separate. Local Docker verification passed `stream1_transformer_cuda_tests`, `contract_tests`, and `dispatcher_cuda_tests`; Kaggle 2xT4 v8 against commit `3bf864a` improved best T4 throughput from `408179.9/405676.1` to `446292.4/448865.3` candidates/s. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_ff1_fused_2026-07-03.md` and `test_results/stream1_transformer_kaggle_benchmark_v8_ff1_fused_2026-07-03.md`.

- Fused the Stream1 piece-transformer QKV projection bias into the QKV GEMM epilogue in `cuda/stream1_transformer.cu`, using the same transformer-only CUTLASS broadcast epilogue pattern with identity activation. The attention path now receives pre-biased QKV, and the SM80 BF16 FMHA pack path was updated to avoid double-adding bias. Removed the now-dead separate QKV bias kernel. MLP Stream1 remains untouched. Local Docker verification passed `stream1_transformer_cuda_tests`, `contract_tests`, and `dispatcher_cuda_tests`; local smoke reached `617951.1` candidates/s at `B_MICRO=512, concurrency=1`. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_qkv_fused_2026-07-03.md`.
- Ran Kaggle 2xT4 benchmark version 9 against commit `83c95ea` after fusing QKV bias into the transformer QKV GEMM epilogue. Best T4 throughput improved from FF1-only v8 `446292.4/448865.3` to `474114.7/467352.5` candidates/s, and from v7 baseline `408179.9/405676.1` to `474114.7/467352.5` candidates/s. The backend remains below the PyTorch transformer reference (`630697.0` candidates/s batch_process), so the next gap is still attention/layout/remaining epilogue fusion. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_kaggle_benchmark_v9_qkv_fused_2026-07-03.md`.
- Added a graph replay timing mode to `tools/stream_benchmark_transformer.cu` via `BEAM_STREAM1_TRANSFORMER_GRAPH_BENCH=1`. The mode captures one Stream1 transformer inference graph per stream and times graph launches, matching the dispatcher/solver launch model more closely than the old host-enqueue benchmark path. Docker verification passed `stream1_transformer_cuda_tests`, `dispatcher_cuda_tests`, and `contract_tests`; local RTX graph replay reached `582514.8` candidates/s at `B_MICRO=1024, concurrency=2` using the v9 transformer weights. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_graph_benchmark_mode_2026-07-03.md`.
- Prepared `kaggle_t4_transformer_benchmark/t4-transformer-stream1-benchmark.ipynb` for graph replay validation by pinning the cloned repository to tag `stream1-transformer-graph-bench-e35432d` at commit `e35432dc475e3eb5b900b0470fe57f72e07f3d18` and passing `BEAM_STREAM1_TRANSFORMER_GRAPH_BENCH=1` into each per-GPU benchmark process. Kaggle submission is currently blocked by the account GPU batch session limit, not by repository code or notebook JSON validation.
- Profiled Stream1 piece-transformer graph replay with Nsight Systems `--cuda-graph-trace=node` and rejected a cuBLASLt-only QKV experiment. The graph-node profile shows remaining time is dominated by transformer linear GEMMs, LayerNorm/copy, and attention layout kernels; cuBLASLt QKV reduced local graph throughput from `582514.8` to `354878.7` candidates/s, so no cuBLASLt runtime path was kept. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_graph_profile_2026-07-03.md`.

- Added a transformer-local fp16 `cols == 256` bias-add fast path in `cuda/stream1_transformer.cu`. The specialized `half2` kernel replaces the generic dtype loop for the T4 fp16 transformer shape only; BF16/non-256 shapes still use the generic path and the MLP Stream1 runtime is untouched. Docker verification passed `stream1_transformer_cuda_tests`, `dispatcher_cuda_tests`, and `contract_tests`. Nsight graph profiling showed the bias-add share drop from about `7.0%` / `289 us` average to `3.7%` / `152 us` average. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_biasadd256_2026-07-03.md`.

- Ran Kaggle 2xT4 benchmark version 15 against `stream1-transformer-biasadd256-b34bf7c` / commit `b34bf7c`. The fp16 256-wide transformer bias-add fast path improved best aggregate throughput from v14 `1139725.9` to `1213776.7` candidates/s (`1.065x`), with best per-GPU rows `614318.7` and `599458.0` candidates/s. This reaches about `0.962x` of the two-T4 PyTorch transformer reference aggregate. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_kaggle_benchmark_v15_biasadd256_2026-07-04.md`.

- Fused Stream1 piece-transformer projection bias with the following LayerNorm step. Attention-output bias is now applied inside LN2, FF2 bias is delayed into the next block's LN1, and the final FF2 bias is applied inside the CLS output LayerNorm. This removes standalone transformer bias-add graph nodes from the main forward loop while leaving the MLP path untouched. Docker verification passed SM86 `stream1_transformer_cuda_tests`, `dispatcher_cuda_tests`, `contract_tests`, plus SM75 `stream1_transformer_cuda_tests`. Local graph replay reached `750833.0` candidates/s at `512x2` and `775386.2` candidates/s under Nsight at `1024x1`; standalone bias-add kernels disappeared from the graph. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_bias_layernorm_2026-07-04.md`.

- Ran Kaggle 2xT4 benchmark version 16 against `stream1-transformer-bias-ln-65bf43d` / commit `65bf43d`. The fused bias+LayerNorm path was valid but did not improve T4 aggregate throughput: best rows were `623236.4` and `587309.8` candidates/s, aggregate `1210546.2`, about `0.997x` of v15 BiasAdd256. The change remains useful local/SM86 evidence but is not a T4 speed win; next transformer work should target GEMM/epilogue or attention layout. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_kaggle_benchmark_v16_bias_ln_2026-07-04.md`.

- Added the first exact-shape Stream1 piece-transformer `block51` backend for p900 (`seq=51,d_model=256,nhead=8,head_dim=32,layers=4,ff_dim=1024`). The backend routes exact p900 shapes through `stream1_transformer_inference_block51_cuda`, fuses input token construction with input LayerNorm, and keeps the measured-better T4 `biasadd256` block schedule rather than the v16 fused bias+LayerNorm schedule. SM86 verification passed `stream1_transformer_cuda_tests`, `dispatcher_cuda_tests`, and `contract_tests`; SM75 verification passed `stream1_transformer_cuda_tests`. Local graph replay reached `753966.2` candidates/s at `512x2`; Nsight shows the fused input+LN kernel replacing the old separate build-input stage while GEMM/epilogue remains dominant. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_block51_2026-07-04.md`.
- Ran Kaggle 2xT4 benchmark version 17 against `stream1-transformer-block51-0d86115` / commit `0d86115`. The exact-shape `block51` backend compiled and ran correctly, but it was not a T4 speed win: best rows were `598701.1` and `592691.2` candidates/s, aggregate `1191392.3`, which is `0.9816x` of v15 `biasadd256` and `0.9842x` of v16. To avoid a hidden regression, `block51` is now an explicit opt-in route via `BEAM_STREAM1_TRANSFORMER_BLOCK51=1`; non-matching shapes still throw when requested. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_kaggle_benchmark_v17_block51_2026-07-04.md`.

- Ran Kaggle 2xT4 benchmark version 18 against `stream1-transformer-block51-optin-7adbe14` / commit `7adbe14` with `BEAM_STREAM1_TRANSFORMER_BLOCK51=1`. The final opt-in route compiled and ran correctly: best rows were `610697.3` and `601347.2` candidates/s, aggregate `1212044.5`. This is `0.9986x` of the best v15 `biasadd256` aggregate and `1.0012x` of v16, so `block51` stays explicit opt-in rather than default. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_kaggle_benchmark_v18_block51_optin_2026-07-04.md`.

- Expanded the Stream1 piece-transformer benchmark sweep to include intermediate T4 points B_MICRO={256,384,512,768,1024,1536,2048,3072,4096} and concurrency={1,2,3,4,5,6} before deeper CUDA rewrites. This is benchmark-only and does not change production inference. Docker smoke with B_MICRO=384, concurrency=3, BEAM_STREAM1_TRANSFORMER_BLOCK51=1 completed. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_extended_sweep_smoke_2026-07-04.md`.
- Archived the native Stream1 piece-transformer optimization track after Kaggle 2xT4 v19 extended sweep. The best verified native/CUTLASS block51 point reached 1224735.7 aggregate candidates/s, about 0.971x of the PyTorch batch_process aggregate, so the next transformer production direction is an explicit C++ LibTorch execution backend for the same `piece_transformer` weight contract. The unverified `BLOCK51_BIAS_LN` native experiment is saved under `docs/archive/stream1_transformer_native/` and removed from the working CUDA source. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_kaggle_benchmark_v19_extended_sweep_2026-07-04.md`.
- Added an opt-in C++ LibTorch Stream1 piece-transformer benchmark target, `stream1_transformer_libtorch_benchmark`, behind `BEAM_ENABLE_LIBTORCH_STREAM1=ON`. The tool loads the same exported `piece_transformer` fast tables as the Python Torch reference, reproduces the token build, LayerNorm/pre-norm transformer blocks, LibTorch SDPA attention, SiLU FFN, CLS output, and quantized score-key checksum. Default builds do not search for Torch and the MLP/native CUDA paths remain separate. Verification: LibTorch target builds in `cmz-native-dev:2026-05-26`, CPU forward smoke passes on the reference fp16 weights, and no-LibTorch `contract_tests` passes in `gpu-dev-cutlass-nsight:cuda128-sm120`. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_libtorch_backend_smoke_2026-07-04.md`.
- Removed checked-in/generated Stream1 weight directories, duplicate `_push_*` package snapshots, raw Kaggle output directories, and raw Nsight/Ncu profiling artifacts from the transformer worktree. Markdown reports and source code remain; weights should be fetched or exported on demand instead of living in Git. Added `.gitignore` patterns for root Stream1 weight directories and duplicate push snapshots.
- Split the opt-in C++ LibTorch Stream1 piece-transformer code into a reusable backend scaffold (`tools/stream1_transformer_libtorch_backend.hpp/.cpp`) and a thin benchmark CLI wrapper. The CMake target remains behind `BEAM_ENABLE_LIBTORCH_STREAM1=ON`, so default builds stay Torch-free. Clean-checkout `contract_tests` no longer require root `stream1_weights`; they now generate a tiny synthetic MLP fixture under `test_results/`. Verification passed no-LibTorch `contract_tests=pass` in `gpu-dev-cutlass-nsight:cuda128-sm120` and opt-in `stream1_transformer_libtorch_benchmark` build in `cmz-native-dev:2026-05-26`. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_libtorch_backend_split_2026-07-04.md`.
- Ran private Kaggle 2xT4 C++ LibTorch Stream1 piece-transformer benchmark against commit `d3525a4` and Kaggle model source `vladkuznetsov266/megaminx-qtransformer-1782210824/PyTorch/default/1`. The script exported fp16 `piece_transformer` weights at runtime and built `stream1_transformer_libtorch_benchmark` with `BEAM_ENABLE_LIBTORCH_STREAM1=ON`. Best rows were GPU0 `470229.0` and GPU1 `477283.0` candidates/s, aggregate `947512.0` candidates/s, which is `0.7512x` of the PyTorch `batch_process` 2xT4 reference and `0.7736x` of native/CUTLASS v19. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_libtorch_kaggle_benchmark_v1_2026-07-04.md` and downloaded Kaggle logs under `test_results/kaggle_libtorch_transformer_benchmark_v1_2026-07-04/`. Updated the LibTorch Kaggle benchmark package to clean exported weights before kernel completion on future runs.
- Optimized the explicit C++ LibTorch Stream1 piece-transformer benchmark scaffold: token construction now precomputes active piece indices/positions per slot, the CLI uses `c10::InferenceMode`, and the Kaggle 2xT4 LibTorch package sweeps smaller batch points around the previous best while keeping exported weights temporary. Default no-LibTorch builds remain Torch-free and MLP/native runtime paths are untouched. Verification passed opt-in `stream1_transformer_libtorch_benchmark` build in `cmz-native-dev:2026-05-26` and default `contract_tests=pass` in `gpu-dev-cutlass-nsight:cuda128-sm120`. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_libtorch_fastpath_smoke_2026-07-04.md`.
- Ran Kaggle 2xT4 C++ LibTorch Stream1 piece-transformer benchmark v2 against commit `5202d9a`. The active-index token-build experiment regressed throughput to `917025.0` aggregate candidates/s (`0.9689x` of LibTorch v1, `0.7270x` of the PyTorch batch_process reference), likely due to LibTorch advanced indexing/scatter overhead. The active-index token path was removed again; `InferenceMode` and the broader batch sweep remain for the next dense-token measurement. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_libtorch_kaggle_benchmark_v2_2026-07-04.md` and downloaded logs under `test_results/kaggle_libtorch_transformer_benchmark_v2_2026-07-04/`.
- Ran Kaggle 2xT4 C++ LibTorch Stream1 piece-transformer benchmark v3 against commit `9481ce2` after restoring dense token build while keeping `c10::InferenceMode` and the broader batch sweep. Best rows improved to GPU0 `484660.0` and GPU1 `486948.0` candidates/s, aggregate `971608.0` (`1.0254x` of LibTorch v1, `0.7703x` of PyTorch batch_process, `0.7933x` of native/CUTLASS v19). Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_libtorch_kaggle_benchmark_v3_2026-07-04.md` and downloaded logs under `test_results/kaggle_libtorch_transformer_benchmark_v3_2026-07-04/`.
- Removed explicit Q/K/V `.contiguous()` calls before LibTorch `scaled_dot_product_attention` in the opt-in C++ Stream1 piece-transformer benchmark, matching the Python-style SDPA view path more closely. Dense token build and `c10::InferenceMode` remain; MLP/default native paths are untouched. Local opt-in LibTorch build passed in `cmz-native-dev:2026-05-26`; real speed impact is pending Kaggle 2xT4 v4. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_libtorch_no_qkv_contiguous_smoke_2026-07-04.md`.
- Ran Kaggle 2xT4 C++ LibTorch Stream1 piece-transformer benchmark v4 against commit `8a8cb5f` after removing explicit Q/K/V `.contiguous()` copies before SDPA. Best rows improved to GPU0 `513315.0` and GPU1 `495637.0` candidates/s, aggregate `1008952.0` (`1.0384x` of v3, `1.0648x` of LibTorch v1, `0.7999x` of PyTorch batch_process). Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_libtorch_kaggle_benchmark_v4_2026-07-04.md` and downloaded logs under `test_results/kaggle_libtorch_transformer_benchmark_v4_2026-07-04/`.
- Switched the opt-in C++ LibTorch Stream1 piece-transformer benchmark projections from `matmul + bias` to `at::linear` over transposed HxK weights for QKV, attention output, FF1, FF2, and final output, keeping dense token build, no-QKV-contiguous SDPA, and `c10::InferenceMode`. MLP/default native paths are untouched. Local opt-in LibTorch build passed in `cmz-native-dev:2026-05-26`; real speed impact is pending Kaggle 2xT4 v5. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_libtorch_linear_projection_smoke_2026-07-04.md`.
- Ran Kaggle 2xT4 C++ LibTorch Stream1 piece-transformer benchmark v5 against commit `7487afb` after switching projection calls to `at::linear`. Best rows improved to GPU0 `665466.0` and GPU1 `669009.0` candidates/s, aggregate `1334475.0` (`1.3226x` of v4, `1.0579x` of PyTorch batch_process, `1.0896x` of native/CUTLASS v19). This identifies `at::linear` dispatch as the major missing C++/PyTorch speed match. Score-key checksums differ from the older `matmul + bias` path due to a different FP16 projection kernel/order, so production wiring needs a fixed-state tolerance check against the Python reference. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_libtorch_kaggle_benchmark_v5_2026-07-04.md` and downloaded logs under `test_results/kaggle_libtorch_transformer_benchmark_v5_2026-07-04/`.
- Re-ran default no-LibTorch `contract_tests=pass` in `gpu-dev-cutlass-nsight:cuda128-sm120` after the LibTorch `at::linear` benchmark change, confirming the default/MLP build path remains Torch-free and unaffected.
- Added explicit LibTorch Stream1 benchmark support for transposed-contiguous projection weights and CUDA Graph replay. Projection weights are loaded once as KxH contiguous tensors for `at::linear`; the CLI now has `--cuda-graph`, using static tensors, a non-default CUDA stream, side-stream warmup, and `at::cuda::CUDAGraph` capture/replay. The Kaggle 2xT4 package now benchmarks both eager and cuda_graph modes and attempts a short Nsight Systems graph profile when `nsys` is available. Default no-LibTorch `contract_tests=pass` remains unchanged. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_libtorch_pretranspose_graph_smoke_2026-07-04.md`.
- Ran Kaggle 2xT4 C++ LibTorch Stream1 piece-transformer benchmark v6 against commit `66d43ca` with transposed-contiguous projection weights and explicit CUDA Graph mode. Eager improved to GPU0 `680638.0`, GPU1 `684619.0`, aggregate `1365257.0` (`1.0823x` PyTorch reference, `1.0231x` v5). CUDA Graph capture/replay worked but was slower: aggregate `1316634.0`, graph/eager `0.9644x`. Kaggle has no Nsight Systems (`NSYS_PROFILE_UNAVAILABLE=nsys_not_found`), so timeline profiling must run on a GPU host with `nsys`. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_libtorch_kaggle_benchmark_v6_2026-07-04.md` and `test_results/stream1_transformer_libtorch_cuda_graph_research_2026-07-04.md`.
- Ran Kaggle 2xT4 LibTorch transformer benchmark v7 for the per-slot token-build cache experiment. It regressed performance versus v6: eager `1356848.0` vs `1365257.0` candidates/s, CUDA Graph `1292276.0` vs `1316634.0`, graph/eager `0.9524x`. The slot-cache code was reverted in commit `93bd650`; keep v6/e802183 as the current best verified implementation. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_libtorch_kaggle_benchmark_v7_2026-07-04.md`.
- Ran local Docker Nsight profiling for the C++ LibTorch Stream1 transformer backend using `cmz-native-dev:2026-05-26` with Docker GPU access. The image already had LibTorch and Nsight Systems, so no Dockerfile change was required. On local RTX 3070 Laptop at batch 384, eager reached `578741` candidates/s and CUDA Graph reached `598639` candidates/s (`1.034x` graph/eager); `graph_b384.log` confirms `cudaGraphLaunch` replay. Nsight Systems shows the main costs are cuBLAS Lt GEMMs, FlashAttention, LayerNorm, and elementwise ops; token-build is secondary. NCU connected but could not collect metrics due host driver/Nsight Compute incompatibility. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_libtorch_local_nsys_profile_2026-07-04.md` and `test_results/nsight_libtorch_transformer_2026-07-04/`.

- Profiled the Stream1 piece-transformer LibTorch/cuBLAS and native CUTLASS paths in Docker with Nsight Systems plus compatible Nsight Compute 2025.1.1. The existing native CUTLASS backend is already the correct cuBLAS replacement route and is faster locally: `871235.6` candidates/s at b384/c1 and `991974.7` candidates/s at b512/c2 versus local LibTorch eager `578741`. NCU confirmed both LibTorch cuBLASLt and native CUTLASS use HMMA tensor cores; native CUTLASS kernels average about `40-43%` SM throughput and `62-70%` DRAM throughput, so the remaining bottleneck is epilogue/layout/LayerNorm structure rather than missing tensor-core use. Tried widening QKV/FF1 CUTLASS tile from `128x64x32` to `128x128x32`; it regressed to `957325.9` candidates/s at b512/c2 and was reverted. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_cutlass_vs_libtorch_profile_2026-07-04.md`.
- Added a private Kaggle 2xT4 backend comparison package, `kaggle_t4_transformer_backend_compare`, that benchmarks original PyTorch `batch_process`, Python Torch over exported `piece_transformer` weights, explicit C++ LibTorch eager/CUDA Graph, and native CUTLASS eager/CUDA Graph against the same runtime-exported fp16 transformer weights from `vladkuznetsov266/megaminx-qtransformer-1782210824`. The package clones `codex/stream1-piece-transformer`, prints the checked-out commit, optionally enforces `EXPECTED_COMMIT_PREFIX` when set, builds `stream_benchmark` plus `stream1_transformer_libtorch_benchmark` once, writes rows/summary artifacts under `/kaggle/working`, and cleans temporary exported weights after the run. Local Docker comparison is being recorded under `test_results/stream1_transformer_backend_compare_local_2026-07-04/`.
- Completed the full local plus Kaggle 2xT4 Stream1 transformer backend comparison. Local RTX 3070 Laptop winner was native CUTLASS eager at `1078358.9` candidates/s, with native CUTLASS graph close at `1059577.3`; LibTorch eager reached `823300.0`. Kaggle 2xT4 v3 completed on commit `1b6a9a7`: original PyTorch `batch_process` won at `1353772.5` aggregate candidates/s, C++ LibTorch eager was the fastest exported-weight path at `1247021.0`, LibTorch CUDA Graph reached `1212341.0`, native CUTLASS graph reached `1164436.6`, native CUTLASS eager reached `1128672.0`, and Python Torch over exported weights reached `975414.6`. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_backend_compare_final_2026-07-04.md` and downloaded Kaggle outputs under `test_results/kaggle_transformer_backend_compare_v3_2026-07-04/`.
- Added `tools/stream1_transformer_backends.py` as the canonical explicit backend registry/launcher for Stream1 `piece_transformer` execution paths: `pytorch`, `libtorch`, and `native_cutlass`. Added unit coverage for backend names, modes, and generated commands/env, plus `docs/stream1_transformer_backends.md` and an architecture note declaring that all three paths remain available for parallel development with no fallback behavior.
- Added a Stream1 piece-transformer backend parity gate and matching benchmark output fields. PyTorch, C++ LibTorch, and native CUTLASS benchmark paths now expose `checksum` plus `first_score_keys`; native CUTLASS can use deterministic synthetic states via `BEAM_STREAM1_SYNTHETIC_STATES=1`. Added `tools/stream1_transformer_parity.py` for explicit backend comparison/dry-run planning, updated backend registry tests, and documented the parity contract. Verification passed Python AST checks, backend registry unit tests, exporter unit tests with `PYTHONPATH=.`, backend dry-runs, and parity dry-run. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_backend_parity_contract_2026-07-05.md`.
- Strengthened the Stream1 piece-transformer backend parity contract with `score_key_digest`, a deterministic FNV-1a 64-bit digest over every quantized score key in batch-major, move-major order. PyTorch, C++ LibTorch, and native CUTLASS benchmark outputs now emit the digest; `tools/stream1_transformer_parity.py` treats digest mismatches or missing digests as correctness failures. Added parity comparison unit tests. Verification passed Python AST checks, backend/parity/exporter tests, parity dry-run, Docker no-LibTorch `contract_tests` plus `stream_benchmark` target build, and opt-in LibTorch benchmark target build. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_score_digest_parity_2026-07-05.md`.
- Extended the Stream1 piece-transformer parity runner to compare explicit backend modes (`backend:mode`) and fixed result-line parsing so PyTorch `_report=` diagnostics cannot overwrite the real benchmark row. Real parity still requires `score_key_digest` to be emitted; exact digest equality is now an opt-in strict gate via `--require-exact-digest`, while default cross-backend comparison uses first-row score-key tolerance and reports exact digest agreement. Switched the opt-in C++ LibTorch FFN activation to `at::silu(at::linear(...))`. Real five-mode parity passed for `pytorch`, `libtorch:eager`, `libtorch:cuda_graph`, `native_cutlass:eager`, and `native_cutlass:graph`; a native residual+bias epilogue experiment was rejected after `b512,c2` regression and left out of source. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_backend_modes_libtorch_silu_2026-07-05.md`.
- Ran Kaggle 2xT4 Stream1 transformer backend comparison v5 against commit `1c069c5` after pinning the expected commit in the Kaggle package and fixing native CUTLASS row parsing for the new `checksum`/`score_key_digest` fields. Version 5 completed in `1316.3s`: original PyTorch `batch_process` remained fastest at `1421505.5` aggregate candidates/s; explicit C++ LibTorch eager was the fastest exported-weight backend at `1385290.0` (`1.1109x` v3, `0.9745x` original PyTorch); LibTorch CUDA Graph reached `1357923.0`; native CUTLASS graph reached `1130487.0`; native CUTLASS eager reached `1117222.4`; Python Torch exported reached `982797.1`. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_backend_compare_v5_2026-07-05.md` and downloaded Kaggle outputs under `test_results/kaggle_transformer_backend_compare_v5_2026-07-05/`.
- Ran Kaggle 2xT4 C++ LibTorch Stream1 transformer fine/upper sweeps v8-v10 against commit `dfd3664`. The benchmark package now writes `checked_out_commit` into the JSON summary and compares against the current full-compare v5 PyTorch reference `1421505.5` candidates/s instead of the stale `1261394.0` reference. v10 found the best exported-weight LibTorch point so far: eager batch `384` on both T4s, aggregate `1534314.0` candidates/s (`1.0794x` of original PyTorch batch_process v5). CUDA Graph remains functional but slower than eager in this run (`1493994.0`, graph/eager `0.9737x`). Evidence: `test_results/stream1_libtorch_transformer_2xt4_fine_upper_sweep_2026-07-05.md`.
- Added repeated-pass support to the explicit C++ LibTorch Stream1 transformer benchmark and backend launcher. `stream1_transformer_libtorch_benchmark` now accepts `--passes`, emits `pass=N`, and writes pass-aware CSV rows; `tools/stream1_transformer_backends.py` forwards `--passes` for `libtorch`; the Kaggle LibTorch-only benchmark now runs three repeated passes over a focused T4 batch set and reports mean-best rows. Verification passed backend/parity/exporter Python tests, opt-in LibTorch Docker build, and default no-LibTorch `contract_tests=pass`. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_libtorch_benchmark_passes_smoke_2026-07-05.md`.
- Ran Kaggle 2xT4 C++ LibTorch Stream1 transformer repeated-pass stability benchmark v11 against commit `ee141bc`. Three passes per batch confirmed eager LibTorch `batch=384` as the current stable exported-weight candidate: aggregate mean `1467441.7` candidates/s, best aggregate `1497577.0`, and `1.032x` of the full backend compare v5 original PyTorch `batch_process` reference. CUDA Graph remained slower with aggregate mean `1411365.0` and best aggregate `1442113.0`. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_libtorch_transformer_stability_v11_2026-07-05.md`; raw Kaggle outputs are under `test_results/kaggle_libtorch_transformer_stability_v11_2026-07-05/`.
- Added a Torch-free dispatcher ring-slot launcher hook as the first production integration seam for explicit non-graph Stream1 backends. Default behavior is unchanged: MLP and native piece-transformer paths still use pre-instantiated CUDA Graph ring-slot templates when no hook is supplied. `dispatcher_cuda_tests` now exercises the hook with a graph passthrough launcher and checks hook call counts. Docker verification passed default no-LibTorch `dispatcher_cuda_tests`, `contract_tests`, and `production_runner` build in `gpu-dev-cutlass-nsight:cuda128-sm120`. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_dispatcher_ring_slot_hook_2026-07-05.md`.
- Added an explicit opt-in LibTorch production runner path for Stream1 `piece_transformer`: `production_runner_libtorch_stream1` builds only under `BEAM_ENABLE_LIBTORCH_STREAM1=ON`, and `BEAM_STREAM1_EXECUTOR=libtorch_eager` installs a dispatcher ring-slot launcher that writes LibTorch score keys into the normal score ring and then launches the existing Stream2 hash/goal kernel. Default `production_runner` remains Torch-free and uses the existing CUDA Graph ring-slot templates. The LibTorch path skips native Stream1 device weight/scratch allocation and fails closed for non-`piece_transformer`, uniform mode, or `output_dim != MOVE_COUNT`. Verification built default `production_runner`, `dispatcher_cuda_tests`, and `contract_tests`; CPU `contract_tests=pass`; opt-in `production_runner_libtorch_stream1` and `stream1_transformer_libtorch_benchmark` built in `cmz-native-dev:2026-05-26`. - Added shared Stream1 piece-transformer scratch byte planning and benchmark memory breakdown. Native CUTLASS runtime allocation and transformer benchmark reporting now use the same token/QKV/attention/context/FF/logit byte plan; LibTorch benchmark output reports native-equivalent activation bytes. The LibTorch tool also tolerates legacy manifests without `move_count` by falling back to `output_dim`. Local Docker parity passed across `pytorch`, `libtorch` eager/graph, and `native_cutlass` eager/graph; Kaggle 2xT4 full compare v6 completed on commit `53ddb28` with `libtorch/eager` fastest at `1.332M` aggregate candidates/s. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_static_plan_scratch_accounting_2026-07-05.md` and downloaded outputs under `test_results/kaggle_transformer_backend_compare_v6_2026-07-05/`.
- Optimized the explicit C++ LibTorch Stream1 piece-transformer token-build slice by using `narrow(1, 0, state_len)` for logical state bytes. Local Python backend/parity tests passed, Docker CUDA real-weight parity passed for `pytorch:eager`, `libtorch:eager`, and `libtorch:cuda_graph`, and Kaggle 2xT4 LibTorch-only stability v12 completed on commit `7c25b2e`: eager best aggregate `1535135.0` candidates/s and mean-best `1501530.7`, versus v11 `1497577.0` best and `1467441.7` mean-best. CUDA Graph remained slower than eager; an in-place `addcmul_` token-build attempt was rejected after real-weight graph execution hit an ATen device-side assert. - Added a MEPhI 8xA100 Megaminx launcher for Vlad Kuznetsov's explicit C++ LibTorch `piece_transformer` Stream1 path. The common MEPhI helper now supports optional `CMAKE_PREFIX_PATH`, opt-in `BEAM_ENABLE_LIBTORCH_STREAM1`, and `BEAM_PRODUCTION_RUNNER_PATH` while defaulting to the existing Torch-free `production_runner`. New `hpc/start_8xa100_libtorch_megaminx.sh` builds `production_runner_libtorch_stream1`, exports `BEAM_STREAM1_EXECUTOR=libtorch_eager`, prepares/uses fp16 exported transformer weights, and defaults to puzzle 991, depth 120, beam 900M with the existing 8xA100 manual pipeline parameters. Verification passed Python backend/parity tests, `git diff --check`, and Docker `bash -n`. Evidence: `test_results/mephi_megaminx_libtorch_transformer_launcher_2026-07-06.md`.
- Bundled Vlad Kuznetsov Megaminx `piece_transformer` fp16 exported weights under `weights/megaminx_vlad_transformer_fp16` and updated the MEPhI LibTorch launcher to use them from the checked-out repo by default. The cluster run no longer requires manually locating or staging `BEAM_WEIGHT_DIR`; overrides still work for alternate exported weights.
- Added `hpc/bench_8xa100_megaminx_transformer.sh` for MEPhI 8xA100 40GB Megaminx transformer benchmarking. The script builds both native and LibTorch Stream1 production runners, runs isolated Stream1 checks plus short full-beam smoke/target sweeps, records TSV rows with depth time and GPU memory, and writes `logs/best_megaminx_transformer.env`. The Megaminx launcher now supports `MEGAMINX_STREAM1_BACKEND=libtorch_eager` or `native_cuda_graph` so the benchmark-selected backend can be used for the production run.
- Updated the MEPhI Megaminx transformer benchmark flow to be Stream1-first. `hpc/bench_8xa100_megaminx_transformer.sh` now defaults to isolated Stream1 backend/batch/concurrency benchmarking, writes `logs/best_megaminx_transformer_stream1.env`, and can optionally run one selected 900M target pass via `RUN_SELECTED_900M_AFTER_STREAM1=1` while keeping the established 24-output 900M pipeline config unchanged except for `BEAM_B_MICRO` and `BEAM_STREAM1_CONCURRENCY`. - Fixed the MEPhI Megaminx transformer Stream1 benchmark export/parsing issues found in cluster job 31729: Slurm-friendly `ISOLATED_BATCH_LIST` now avoids comma splitting in `--export`, PyTorch `candidates_per_s` is parsed, and AWK best-env writers no longer use `log` as a variable. - Made the MEPhI Megaminx transformer Stream1 isolated benchmark a full backend-family grid: PyTorch eager, LibTorch eager, LibTorch CUDA Graph, and native CUTLASS graph now all sweep `B_MICRO=512 1024 2048 4096 8192 12288 16384` against concurrency `1 2 4 8`. PyTorch/LibTorch benchmark tools now accept `--concurrency`, report `rows_per_launch_group`, and compute throughput from `batch * concurrency * iters * move_count`; the MEPhI parser now sees both `candidates_per_sec=` and PyTorch `candidates_per_s=` rows. Production best-env selection still only picks production-capable native graph or LibTorch eager rows.
- Updated `hpc/solve_then_reflect.sh` so Megaminx original+reflected jobs can use the explicit Stream1 transformer backend and SLURM array puzzle ids. The launcher now defaults `BEAM_WEIGHT_DIR` to the committed Vlad transformer export when present, configures `native_cuda_graph` or `libtorch_eager` explicitly, and preserves the original/reflected history cleanup flow. - Added explicit `BEAM_STREAM1_EXECUTOR=native_eager` / `native_no_graph` production support for native CUDA/CUTLASS Stream1 ring slots. This avoids pre-instantiating thousands of Stream1/2 ring-slot CUDA Graph execs for tiny transformer batches such as `B_MICRO=512`, while leaving Stream3/4/5/final graph behavior unchanged. Also fixed LibTorch ring-slot Stream2 indexing to use job-relative `ring=0, ring_slot=0`, and updated MEPhI launchers to accept `MEGAMINX_STREAM1_BACKEND=native_eager`. Verification built `production_runner`, ran `contract_tests=pass`, and passed Docker `bash -n` for MEPhI scripts. Evidence: `test_results/mephi_megaminx_transformer_native_eager_2026-07-06.md`.- Added bounded native CUDA Graph windowing for Stream1 piece-transformer runs via `BEAM_RING_GRAPH_EXECS_PER_LANE`. The runtime caps derived physical `ring_count` when the env var is set, so graph execution resources stay bounded while the dispatcher continues reusing physical rings over the full frontier. Added `native_windowed_graph` / `native_graph_window` launcher aliases with a default cap of 32 graph execs per lane, and production logs now print the effective runtime ring/graph window. Verification: Docker build of `production_runner`, `contract_tests=pass`, launcher `bash -n`. Evidence: `test_results/mephi_megaminx_transformer_windowed_graph_2026-07-06.md`.
- Reworked Stream1 PieceTransformer windowed CUDA Graph mode so `BEAM_RING_GRAPH_EXECS_PER_LANE` caps only the number of instantiated Stream1+Stream2 graph templates, not physical `ring_count` or Stream3 ring slots. Windowed graph templates now use a device `ring_slot_job_index` descriptor; Stream1 input/output and Stream2 hash kernels read the physical job index at launch time and write into the full ring buffers. Scheduler reuse is guarded with per-template completion events. Verification: Docker build of `production_runner`, `contract_tests=pass`, and compile of `stream1_transformer_cuda_tests`, `stream2_cuda_tests`, `dispatcher_cuda_tests`. - Added a dispatcher-backed Stream1+2+3 graph smoke benchmark. `run_depth_cuda_graphs` now has an explicit `DepthDispatchStopStage` with default `Full`, plus `AfterStream12` and `AfterStream3` for benchmark-only early exits before Stream4/5/final. Added `tools/stream_pipeline_benchmark.cu`, which uses the real static-memory, dispatcher, Stream1 piece-transformer native graph, Stream2 hash/goal, and optional Stream3 graph paths in single-process mode. Extended `hpc/bench_8xa100_megaminx_transformer.sh` with `RUN_PIPELINE_SMOKE=1` and TSV output for `stream12/stream123` graph-window sweeps. Production defaults remain unchanged. - Fixed the new Stream1+2+3 pipeline smoke benchmark reset path after MEPhI cluster job 31743 hit `cudaMemset(memory.streams.current_threshold_active_index)=invalid argument`. The tool now follows the existing stream microbenchmark pattern: allocate benchmark-owned threshold buffers, attach them to `StaticDeviceMemory`, instantiate/use graphs with those pointers, then free the buffers before static memory teardown. Production runner/static layout defaults remain unchanged. Verification: Docker rebuilt `stream_pipeline_benchmark`, rebuilt `contract_tests`, and `contract_tests=pass`. - Added a private Kaggle 2xT4 pipeline smoke package, `kaggle_t4_pipeline_smoke`, for the dispatcher-backed Stream1+2+3 graph smoke benchmark. - Fixed the Stream1+2+3 pipeline smoke benchmark after MEPhI cluster job 31745 failed at `cudaMemset(memory.streams.threshold_request_local)=invalid argument`. `BenchmarkThresholdBuffers` now owns and attaches `threshold_request_local/global` in addition to the threshold triple, so the benchmark reset path no longer touches invalid static scratch pointers.
